7.6 Full preservation under color reversal
Define new notation by
𝑖′ = 𝑖, 𝑗 ′ = 𝑘, 𝑘 ′ = 𝑗,
𝑎′
𝑖 = 𝑏𝑖, 𝑏 ′
𝑖 = 𝑎𝑖, 𝑎 ′
𝑗 = 𝑏𝑘, 𝑏 ′
𝑗 = 𝑎𝑘, 𝑎 ′
𝑘 = 𝑏𝑗, 𝑏 ′
𝑘 = 𝑎𝑗
The scalar parameters transform as
𝜆′ = 𝜆, 𝛿 ′ = 𝛽, 𝛽 ′ = 𝛿, 𝑔 ′ = 𝛼, 𝛼 ′ = 𝑔,
𝑃′ = 𝑃 , 𝑅 ′ = 𝑇 , 𝑇 ′ = 𝑅, 𝑑 ′ = 𝑑, 𝑆 ′ = 𝐶, 𝐶 ′ = 𝑆.
Under these replacements, every HCR equation is merely renamed. The same element 𝑊 is still
(𝑃′ − 1)𝑛𝑖′ + (𝑅′ − 1)𝑛𝑗′ + (𝑇′ − 1)𝑛𝑘′
The semigroup Γ, 𝐹, and 𝑚 are unchanged.
The actual pseudo-Frobenius gaps are exchanged by
𝑞′
𝐴 = 𝑞𝐵, 𝑞 ′
𝐵 = 𝑞𝐴, 𝑞 ′
𝐽 = 𝑞𝐾, 𝑞 ′
𝐾 = 𝑞𝐽
Hence all four actuality and gap statuses are preserved. PFIBER is the same nonnegative factorization of
the same element 𝑃 𝑛𝑖, with 𝑡′ = 𝑢, 𝑢′ = 𝑡.
ROOT0 becomes
𝑚 + 𝑑′𝑛𝑖′ = 𝑆′𝑛𝑗′ + 𝐶′𝑛𝑘′,
In the case under consideration,
1 ≤ 𝑑′ ≤ 𝜆′, 𝑆 ′ = 𝐶 ≥ 𝛼 + 1 = 𝑔 ′ + 1, 𝐶 ′ = 𝑆 = 𝑔 = 𝛼 ′.
Thus all hypotheses of UNITROOT hold for the same configuration after relabeling.
7.7 Input from CORE to the late-stage closure
7.8 Minimal sufficient CORE input
Assume the following.
1. A numerical semigroup Γ = ⟨𝑚, 𝑛𝑖, 𝑛𝑗, 𝑛𝑘⟩, with 0 < 𝑚 < min(𝑛𝑖, 𝑛𝑗, 𝑛𝑘), and Frobenius number
𝐹 ∉ Γ .
2. Positive integers 𝜆, 𝛿, 𝛽, 𝑔, 𝛼, 𝑎𝑗, 𝑏𝑗, 𝑎𝑘, 𝑏𝑘, with
𝑎𝑖 = 𝜆 + 𝛿, 𝑏 𝑖 = 𝜆 + 𝛽, 𝜌 𝑟 = 𝑎𝑟 + 𝑏𝑟, 𝑃 = 𝜆 + 𝛿 + 𝛽, 𝑅 = 𝑎 𝑗 + 𝑔, 𝑇 = 𝑏 𝑘 + 𝛼.
The genuine critical relations in the pure tail 𝐻 = ⟨𝑛𝑖, 𝑛𝑗, 𝑛𝑘⟩ are
𝜌𝑖𝑛𝑖 = 𝑏𝑗𝑛𝑗 + 𝑎𝑘𝑛𝑘, 𝜌 𝑗𝑛𝑗 = 𝑎𝑖𝑛𝑖 + 𝑏𝑘𝑛𝑘, 𝜌 𝑘𝑛𝑘 = 𝑏𝑖𝑛𝑖 + 𝑎𝑗𝑛𝑗.
Here “critical” means that 𝜌𝑟 is the least positive multiple of 𝑛𝑟 admitting a nonnegative representation
by the other two generators. No minimality is assumed for arbitrary signed relations.
3. One and the same actual element
𝑊 = 𝐹 + 𝑚 = (𝑃 − 1)𝑛𝑖 + (𝑅 − 1)𝑛𝑗 + (𝑇 − 1)𝑛𝑘.

4. Four pseudo-Frobenius gaps in the same configuration:
𝑞𝐴𝑖
= (𝑃 − 1)𝑛𝑖 + (𝑎𝑗 − 1)𝑛𝑗 − 𝑛𝑘,
𝑞𝐵𝑖

= (𝑎𝑖 − 1)𝑛𝑖 − 𝑛𝑗 + (𝑇 − 1)𝑛𝑘.
What is used below is that each 𝑞 ∉ Γ and that the successor in the displayed missing direction belongs
to Γ. The latter follows from the pseudo-Frobenius property.

𝑚 + 𝑑𝑛𝑖 = 𝑆𝑛𝑗 + 𝐶𝑛𝑘, 1 ≤ 𝑑 ≤ 𝜆, 𝑆 ≥ 𝑔 + 1, 𝐶 ≥ 𝛼. (CORE-ROOT)
Here 𝐶 is the coefficient in ROOT and is distinct from 𝑇 = 𝑏 𝑘 + 𝛼. We do not assume minimality or
equality of any return levels.
Input lemma. The configuration of § 7.1 satisfies this CORE input. We now derive the consequences
required in §8.1–§10.1.
7.9 Missing-coordinate returns and positive m-levels follow from CORE
By the pseudo-Frobenius property, every relevant successor lies in Γ. If a genuine factorization of such a
successor contained one copy of its missing generator, removing that copy coefficientwise would put the
original gap in Γ. Thus every missing coordinate is zero in every such factorization.
First write
𝐸𝐴 = 𝑞𝐴𝑖
+ 𝑛𝑖 = 𝐿𝑖𝑚 + 𝑈𝑗𝑛𝑗 + 𝑈𝑘𝑛𝑘
Suppose 𝐿𝑖 = 0. Comparing with the canonical row gives
𝑃 𝑛𝑖 = (𝑈𝑗 − 𝑎𝑗 + 1)𝑛𝑗 + (𝑈𝑘 + 1)𝑛𝑘.
Since 𝑃 < 𝜌 𝑖, if the 𝑗-coefficient is nonnegative this is an immediate subcritical 𝑖-contradiction. If it is
negative, then
(𝑈𝑘 + 1)𝑛𝑘 = 𝑃 𝑛𝑖 + (𝑎𝑗 − 1 − 𝑈𝑗)𝑛𝑗
so 𝑈𝑘 + 1 ≥ 𝜌𝑘. Comparing one copy of ℛ𝑘 gives
𝛿𝑛𝑖 = (𝑈𝑗 + 1)𝑛𝑗 + (𝑈𝑘 + 1 − 𝜌𝑘)𝑛𝑘,
again a positive subcritical 𝑖-contradiction. Hence 𝐿𝑖 ≥ 1.
Similarly, if 𝐸𝐵 = 𝑞𝐵𝑖
+ 𝑛𝑖 = 𝐿𝑏𝑚 + 𝑉𝑗𝑛𝑗 + 𝑉𝑘𝑛𝑘 had 𝐿𝑏 = 0, then
𝑃 𝑛𝑖 = (𝑉𝑗 + 1)𝑛𝑗 + (𝑉𝑘 − 𝑏𝑘 + 1)𝑛𝑘
When necessary, comparing one copy of ℛ𝑗 produces
𝛽𝑛𝑖 = (𝑉𝑗 + 1 − 𝜌𝑗)𝑛𝑗 + (𝑉𝑘 + 1)𝑛𝑘
which is impossible. Thus 𝐿𝑏 ≥ 1. Every use of criticality here occurs in a relation with m-coordinate zero.
From the CORE representation of 𝑊 and the four rows, the complements are exactly
𝑐𝐴𝑖
= 𝑔𝑛𝑗 + 𝑇 𝑛𝑘, 𝑐 𝐵𝑖
= 𝑅𝑛𝑗 + 𝛼𝑛𝑘, 𝑐 𝐵𝑗
= 𝛿𝑛𝑖 + 𝑇 𝑛𝑘, 𝑐 𝐴𝑘
= 𝛽𝑛𝑖 + 𝑅𝑛𝑗.
Add the corresponding complement to EA or EB and remove one actually present copy of 𝑚. This gives
𝐹 + 𝑛𝑖 = (𝐿𝑖 − 1)𝑚 + (𝑈𝑗 + 𝑔)𝑛𝑗 + (𝑈𝑘 + 𝑇 )𝑛𝑘,
40

PAGE 42
Canonical reduction and type T ype bound under canonical reduction
𝐹 + 𝑛𝑖 = (𝐿𝑏 − 1)𝑚 + (𝑉𝑗 + 𝑅)𝑛𝑗 + (𝑉𝑘 + 𝛼)𝑛𝑘.
If the 𝑗- or 𝑘-coefficient reached the corresponding 𝜌, replace one canonical packet to create a positive copy
of 𝑛𝑖; removing that copy would give 𝐹 ∈ Γ . Hence
𝑈𝑗 + 𝑔 < 𝜌𝑗, 𝑈 𝑘 + 𝑇 < 𝜌 𝑘, 𝑉 𝑗 + 𝑅 < 𝜌𝑗, 𝑉 𝑘 + 𝛼 < 𝜌𝑘. (FI-CAPS)
In particular, 𝑅 < 𝜌𝑗, 𝑇 < 𝜌 𝑘, 𝑔 < 𝑏𝑗, and 𝛼 < 𝑎𝑘.
Next consider 𝑄𝑗 = 𝑞𝐵𝑗
+ 𝑛𝑗 = 𝐿𝑗𝑚 + 𝐴𝑗𝑛𝑖 + 𝐶𝑗𝑛𝑘. If 𝐿𝑗 = 0, then
(𝑏𝑖 − 1 − 𝐴𝑗)𝑛𝑖 + 𝑅𝑛𝑗 = (𝐶𝑗 + 1)𝑛𝑘.
If the 𝑖-coefficient is nonpositive, this contradicts 𝑅 < 𝜌𝑗. If it is positive, 𝑘-criticality forces 𝐶𝑗 + 1 ≥ 𝜌 𝑘.
Comparing one copy of ℛ𝑘 then gives
𝑔𝑛𝑗 = (𝐴𝑗 + 1)𝑛𝑖 + (𝐶𝑗 + 1 − 𝜌𝑘)𝑛𝑘,
a subcritical 𝑗-contradiction. Thus 𝐿𝑗 ≥ 1. Dually, assuming 𝐿𝑘 = 0 in 𝑄𝑘 = 𝑞𝐴𝑘
+ 𝑛𝑘 = 𝐿𝑘𝑚 + 𝐴𝑘𝑛𝑖 +
𝐵𝑘𝑛𝑗 reduces to
𝛼𝑛𝑘 = (𝐴𝑘 + 1)𝑛𝑖 + (𝐵𝑘 + 1 − 𝜌𝑗)𝑛𝑗
which is impossible. Hence 𝐿𝑘 ≥ 1.
We refer to these four positive-level actual representations as RET. They all follow from CORE.
7.10 Additional caps and slopes
Adding the complement to the actual row 𝑄𝑗 and removing one copy of 𝑚 gives
𝐹 + 𝑛𝑗 = (𝐿𝑗 − 1)𝑚 + (𝐴𝑗 + 𝛿)𝑛𝑖 + (𝐶𝑗 + 𝑇 )𝑛𝑘. (FJ)
If 𝐶𝑗 + 𝑇 ≥ 𝜌 𝑘, replacing one ℛ𝑘-packet creates 𝑎𝑗 ≥ 1 copies of 𝑛𝑗, and removing one gives 𝐹 ∈ Γ .
Therefore
𝐶𝑗 + 𝑇 < 𝜌 𝑘. (FJ-KCAP)
In particular, 𝐶𝑗 < 𝜌𝑘.
Make one completed ROOT substitution into Qj:
𝑄𝑗 = (𝐿𝑗 − 1)𝑚 + (𝐴𝑗 − 𝑑)𝑛𝑖 + 𝑆𝑛𝑗 + (𝐶𝑗 + 𝐶)𝑛𝑘.
If 𝐴𝑗 ≥ 𝑑 , every coefficient is nonnegative and the representation contains a positive 𝑗-coefficient, contra-
dicting the gap property. Thus 𝐴𝑗 ≤ 𝑑 − 1. The same argument gives 𝐴𝑘 ≤ 𝑑 − 1. Inserting ROOT and one
copy of ℛ𝑗 or ℛ𝑘 into EA/EB also yields
𝑈𝑗 + 𝑆 < 𝜌 𝑗, 𝑈 𝑘 + 𝐶 < 𝜌 𝑘, 𝑉 𝑗 + 𝑆 < 𝜌 𝑗, 𝑉 𝑘 + 𝐶 < 𝜌 𝑘
For example, in each of the following four representations, nonnegativity of the indicated problematic coef-
ficient would put the original gap in Γ:
𝑞𝐴𝑖
= (𝐿𝑖 − 1)𝑚 + (𝑎𝑖 − 𝑑 − 1)𝑛𝑖 + (𝑈𝑗 + 𝑆 − 𝜌𝑗)𝑛𝑗 + (𝑈𝑘 + 𝐶 + 𝑏𝑘)𝑛𝑘,
𝑞𝐴𝑖
= (𝐿𝑖 − 1)𝑚 + (𝑏𝑖 − 𝑑 − 1)𝑛𝑖 + (𝑈𝑗 + 𝑆 + 𝑎𝑗)𝑛𝑗 + (𝑈𝑘 + 𝐶 − 𝜌𝑘)𝑛𝑘,
𝑞𝐵𝑖
= (𝐿𝑏 − 1)𝑚 + (𝑎𝑖 − 𝑑 − 1)𝑛𝑖 + (𝑉𝑗 + 𝑆 − 𝜌𝑗)𝑛𝑗 + (𝑉𝑘 + 𝐶 + 𝑏𝑘)𝑛𝑘,
𝑞𝐵𝑖
= (𝐿𝑏 − 1)𝑚 + (𝑏𝑖 − 𝑑 − 1)𝑛𝑖 + (𝑉𝑗 + 𝑆 + 𝑎𝑗)𝑛𝑗 + (𝑉𝑘 + 𝐶 − 𝜌𝑘)𝑛𝑘.
Before removing one copy of 𝑛𝑖, the relevant coefficient is 𝑎𝑖 − 𝑑 > 0 or 𝑏𝑖 − 𝑑 > 0.
41

PAGE 43
Canonical reduction and type T ype bound under canonical reduction
Comparison of ROOT with ℛ𝑗 gives
𝜌𝑗𝑚 = (𝑆𝑎𝑖 − 𝑑𝜌𝑗)𝑛𝑖 + (𝑆𝑏𝑘 + 𝐶𝜌𝑗)𝑛𝑘.
If 𝑑𝜌𝑗 − 𝑆𝑎𝑖 ≤ 0, then 𝑚 > 𝑛𝑘, contradicting multiplicity. Similarly,
𝜌𝑘𝑚 = (𝐶𝑏𝑖 − 𝑑𝜌𝑘)𝑛𝑖 + (𝑆𝜌𝑘 + 𝐶𝑎𝑗)𝑛𝑗
gives the second slope inequality. Hence
𝑑𝜌𝑗 − 𝑆𝑎𝑖 > 0, 𝑑𝜌 𝑘 − 𝐶𝑏𝑖 > 0. (SLOPES)
In particular, 𝑆 < 𝜌𝑗, 𝐶 < 𝜌𝑘. This argument uses multiplicity, not criticality.
7.11 T wo intrinsic candidates and safe color reversal
Set
ℎ = ⌊𝜆/𝑑⌋, 𝑞 0 = ℎ + 1, 𝑟 = 𝜆 − ℎ𝑑, 𝑒 0 = 𝑞0𝑑 − 𝜆 = 𝑑 − 𝑟.
Using ROOT and −ℛ𝑖, the completed F-row for arbitrary integers 𝑥, 𝑦is
𝑣(𝑥, 𝑦) = (𝑥 − 1, 𝑃 − 1 + 𝑥𝑑 − 𝑦𝜌𝑖, 𝑅 − 1 − 𝑥𝑆 + 𝑦𝑏𝑗, 𝑇 − 1 − 𝑥𝐶 + 𝑦𝑎𝑘).
If the final row is nonnegative, then 𝑥 ≥ 1 . A successful point with 𝑦 ≤ 0 requires the 𝑗, 𝑘-coordinates to
succeed already at (𝑥, 𝑦) = (1, 0), in which case that point itself gives a nonnegative representation of 𝐹.
For 𝑦 ≥ 1, the 𝑖-fit condition is
𝑥𝑑 ≥ 𝜆 + 1 + (𝑦 − 1)𝜌𝑖.
The identity
𝜌𝑖 − 𝑞0𝑑 − 𝑞0 = (𝑞0 − 2)(𝑑 − 1) + 2𝑟 + 𝛿 + 𝛽 − 2 ≥ 0 (RHO-GAP)
then gives 𝑥 ≥ 𝑦𝑞0. Thus for the second candidate
𝑣(𝑞0, 1) = (𝑞0 − 1, 𝑒0 − 1, 𝐽0, 𝐾0),
𝐽0 = 𝜌𝑗 + 𝑔 − 1 − 𝑞0𝑆, 𝐾 0 = 𝜌𝑘 + 𝛼 − 1 − 𝑞0𝐶
if either the 𝑗- or 𝑘-coordinate is negative, then no point in the region 𝑦 ≥ 1 succeeds. Conversely, if both are
nonnegative, the second candidate succeeds. This gives the complete integer test by two candidates.
Since 𝐹 is a gap, the second candidate fails. Moreover,
𝑞0𝑚 = (𝜌𝑖 − 𝑞0𝑑)𝑛𝑖 + (𝑞0𝑆 − 𝑏𝑗)𝑛𝑗 + (𝑞0𝐶 − 𝑎𝑘)𝑛𝑘 (QM)
together with RHO-GAP shows that the 𝑗- and 𝑘-coordinates cannot both be deficient. More strongly,
𝐽0 < 0 ⟹ 𝑞 0𝐶 ≤ 𝑎𝑘 − 1, 𝐾 0 < 0 ⟹ 𝑞 0𝑆 ≤ 𝑏𝑗 − 1.
For example, in the first implication, if 𝑞0𝐶 ≥ 𝑎𝑘, then in QM the 𝑖-coefficient is ≥ 𝑞0 and the 𝑗-coefficient
is ≥ 𝑅 > 0, forcing 𝑚 > 𝑛𝑖.
If only 𝐾0 is negative, interchange 𝑗, 𝑘and simultaneously apply
(𝑎𝑖, 𝑏𝑖) ↦ (𝑏𝑖, 𝑎𝑖), (𝑎𝑗, 𝑏𝑗) ↦ (𝑏𝑘, 𝑎𝑘), (𝑎𝑘, 𝑏𝑘) ↦ (𝑏𝑗, 𝑎𝑗).
Thus
(𝛿, 𝛽, 𝑔, 𝛼, 𝑅, 𝑇 , 𝑆, 𝐶) ↦ (𝛽, 𝛿, 𝛼, 𝑔, 𝑇 , 𝑅, 𝐶, 𝑆).
The four pseudo-Frobenius rows are exchanged by𝑞𝐴𝑖
↔ 𝑞𝐵𝑖
and 𝑞𝐵𝑗
↔ 𝑞𝐴𝑘
, while Γ, 𝐹 , 𝑚, 𝑊 , 𝜆, 𝑑remain
unchanged.
42

PAGE 44
Canonical reduction and type T ype bound under canonical reduction
Before reversal, ℎ𝐶 < 𝜌𝑘 and 𝐾0 < 0 imply
1 ≤ −𝐾0 ≤ 𝐶 − 𝛼,
so 𝐶 ≥ 𝛼 + 1. After reversal, 𝑆′ ≥ 𝑔′ + 1 still holds and 𝐶′ = 𝑆 ≥ 𝛼 ′. Hence the whole CORE package is
preserved.
We may therefore orient the situation so that 𝐽0 < 0. Put
Δ0 = 𝑞0𝑆 − 𝜌𝑗 − 𝑔 + 1 ≥ 1, 𝐴 0 = 𝑒0 − 𝛿 − 1 ≥ 0, 𝐶 0 = 𝑎𝑘 − 𝑞0𝐶 − 1 ≥ 0.
The sign of 𝐴0 follows because 𝑞0𝑆 > 𝜌𝑗, together with SLOPES, implies 𝑞0𝑑 > 𝑎𝑖.
𝜏0 = 𝜌𝑗 − ℎ𝑆 ≥ 1, Δ 0 + 𝜏0 = 𝑆 − 𝑔 + 1, 1 ≤ Δ 0 ≤ 𝑆 − 𝑔.
The second candidate gives the genuine actual relation
Ω0 = 𝐹 + Δ0𝑛𝑗 = (𝑞0 − 1)𝑚 + (𝑒0 − 1)𝑛𝑖 + (𝐶0 + 𝑇 )𝑛𝑘 (COMPACT)
The source 𝑐𝐵𝑗
= 𝛿𝑛𝑖 + 𝑇 𝑛𝑘 is actually contained in this one factorization. Removing it and adding 𝑚yields
𝑞𝐵𝑗
+ Δ0𝑛𝑗 = 𝑞0𝑚 + 𝐴0𝑛𝑖 + 𝐶0𝑛𝑘.
7.12 Making the required lower bounds on return levels explicit
7.12.1 Ai/Bi
Comparing the canonical and actual expressions for EA and eliminating m completely gives
(𝑃 + 𝐿𝑖𝑑)𝑛𝑖 = (𝐿𝑖𝑆 + 𝑈𝑗 − 𝑎𝑗 + 1)𝑛𝑗 + (𝐿𝑖𝐶 + 𝑈𝑘 + 1)𝑛𝑘.
If 𝐿𝑖𝑑 < 𝜆 , the coefficient on the left is below 𝜌𝑖. If the 𝑗-coefficient on the right is nonnegative, this
is immediately a subcritical 𝑖-contradiction. If it is negative, then the 𝑘-coefficient must be ≥ 𝜌 𝑘; after
comparing one copy of ℛ𝑘,
(𝛿 + 𝐿𝑖𝑑)𝑛𝑖 = (𝐿𝑖𝑆 + 𝑈𝑗 + 1)𝑛𝑗 + (𝐿𝑖𝐶 + 𝑈𝑘 + 1 − 𝜌𝑘)𝑛𝑘
which is again a subcritical 𝑖-contradiction. The dual argument for EB is identical. Hence
𝐿𝑖𝑑 ≥ 𝜆, 𝐿 𝑏𝑑 ≥ 𝜆, 𝐿 𝑖, 𝐿𝑏 ≥ ⌈𝜆/𝑑⌉ ≥ ℎ. (I-LEVEL)
7.12.2 Bj
Suppose 𝐿𝑗 ≤ ℎ. Then
(𝑏𝑖 − 1 − 𝐴𝑗 + 𝐿𝑗𝑑)𝑛𝑖 + (𝑅 − 𝐿𝑗𝑆)𝑛𝑗 = (𝐶𝑗 + 𝐿𝑗𝐶 + 1)𝑛𝑘.
The 𝑖-coefficient is positive because 𝐴𝑗 ≤ 𝑑 − 1 , and it is below 𝜌𝑖 because 𝐿𝑗𝑑 ≤ 𝜆 . If the 𝑗-coefficient
is nonpositive, this is a subcritical 𝑖-contradiction. If it is positive, applying 𝑘-criticality and then comparing
ℛ𝑘 gives
(𝐿𝑗𝑑 − 𝐴𝑗 − 1)𝑛𝑖 + (𝑔 − 𝐿𝑗𝑆)𝑛𝑗 = (𝐶𝑗 + 𝐿𝑗𝐶 + 1 − 𝜌𝑘)𝑛𝑘
The 𝑗-coefficient is negative. If the 𝑖-coefficient is nonpositive, positivity fails; if it is positive, we again
obtain a subcritical 𝑖-contradiction. Thus 𝐿𝑗 ≥ 𝑞0.
If 𝐿𝑗 = 𝑞0, compare the same actual element 𝑞𝐵𝑗
+ Δ0𝑛𝑗 and eliminate m:
0 = (𝐴𝑗 − 𝐴0)𝑛𝑖 + (Δ0 − 1)𝑛𝑗 + (𝐶𝑗 − 𝐶0)𝑛𝑘.
The absolute value of each of the three coefficients is below the corresponding𝜌. If the relation were nonzero,
one sign class would consist of a subcritical multiple of a single generator, impossible. Hence
𝐿𝑗 = 𝑞0 ⟹ (Δ0, 𝐴𝑗, 𝐶𝑗) = (1, 𝐴0, 𝐶0).
The subcritical bound on 𝐶𝑗 used here is exactly FJ-KCAP from § 7.10.

PAGE 45
Canonical reduction and type T ype bound under canonical reduction
7.12.3 Ak in the strict region 𝐶 > 𝛼
Assume 𝐿𝑘 ≤ ℎ. Eliminating m gives
(𝑎𝑖 − 1 − 𝐴𝑘 + 𝐿𝑘𝑑)𝑛𝑖 + (𝑇 − 𝐿𝑘𝐶)𝑛𝑘 = (𝐵𝑘 + 𝐿𝑘𝑆 + 1)𝑛𝑗.
The first 𝑖-coefficient is positive and below 𝜌𝑖. If the 𝑘-coefficient is nonpositive, we get a subcritical 𝑖-
contradiction. If it is positive, apply 𝑗-criticality and then compare ℛ𝑗:
(𝐿𝑘𝑑 − 𝐴𝑘 − 1)𝑛𝑖 = (𝐵𝑘 + 𝐿𝑘𝑆 + 1 − 𝜌𝑗)𝑛𝑗 + (𝐿𝑘𝐶 − 𝛼)𝑛𝑘. (K-PURE)
Since 𝐶 > 𝛼 , the 𝑘-coefficient on the right is positive, so both signs of the 𝑖-coefficient are impossible.
Therefore 𝐿𝑘 ≥ 𝑞0. In this range, the 𝑗-coefficient on the right of K-PURE is also positive because 𝑞0𝑆 > 𝜌𝑗,
and hence
𝐿𝑘𝑑 − 𝐴𝑘 − 1 ≥ 𝜌𝑖 ≥ 𝑞0𝑑 + 𝑞0.
Thus in fact 𝐿𝑘 ≥ 𝑞0 + 1.
Insert 𝑞0 copies of ROOT and one 𝑅𝑗 into the actual FK representation, and finally remove one copy of
𝑛𝑘. The completed F-row is
𝐹 = (𝐿𝑘 − 𝑞0 − 1)𝑚 + (𝐴𝑘 + 𝛽 + 𝛿 − 𝑒0)𝑛𝑖
+ (𝐵𝑘 + 𝑅 + 𝑞0𝑆 − 𝜌𝑗)𝑛𝑗 + (𝑞0𝐶 + 𝑏𝑘 − 1)𝑛𝑘.
All coefficients except possibly the 𝑖-coefficient are nonnegative, so
𝐴𝑘 + 𝛽 + 𝛿 ≤ 𝑒0 − 1, 𝑒 0 ≥ 𝛽 + 𝛿 + 1. (SUM-NOTCH)
This strict-region conclusion is not used when 𝐶 = 𝛼.
7.13 The intrinsic packet and the cap from FK
Comparing ℎ copies of ROOT with one 𝑅𝑗 gives the exact equality
ℎ𝑚 + 𝜏0𝑛𝑗 = (𝑎𝑖 − ℎ𝑑)𝑛𝑖 + (𝑏𝑘 + ℎ𝐶)𝑛𝑘. (SHIFT-NEW)
All coefficients are nonnegative, and 𝑎𝑖 − ℎ𝑑 = 𝑟 + 𝛿 > 0. By I-LEVEL, the m-source in EA and EB always
contains at least ℎ copies. Therefore the replacement can be made within those same actual factorizations,
giving
𝑈𝑗, 𝑉𝑗 ≤ 𝜏0 − 1.
In the strict region 𝐶 > 𝛼 , the actual FK representation has m-coefficient 𝐿𝑘 − 1 ≥ ℎ + 1 , so it also
contains the required ℎ copies. If 𝐵𝑘 + 𝑅 ≥ 𝜏0, then
𝐹 = (𝐿𝑘 − ℎ − 1)𝑚 + (𝐴𝑘 + 𝛽 + 𝑎𝑖 − ℎ𝑑)𝑛𝑖
+ (𝐵𝑘 + 𝑅 − 𝜏0)𝑛𝑗 + (𝑏𝑘 + ℎ𝐶 − 1)𝑛𝑘 ∈ Γ.
Hence
0 ≤ 𝐵𝑘 ≤ 𝜏0 − 𝑅 − 1, 𝜏 0 ≥ 𝑅 + 1. (FK-STRONG)
This 𝜏0-cap from FK is used in § 9.6.
8 Boundary elimination and the packet window
We take as input the root and the actual returns obtained in the preceding section. We first specify the selection
set and prove existence in order to define the first fitting point, and then determine the range in which its packet
can be contained within the same element. We separate the boundary case 𝐶 = 𝛼 from the strict case and
show that the final residual range is exhausted by 𝒰 and 𝒟. For ease of reference, the positivity expansions
are also collected in Appendix C.

8.1 First point, boundary, and strict packet window
We use CORE, RET, the caps, and the slopes from § 7.7. In what follows, the local symbols 𝐿, 𝑀denote
𝐿𝑖, 𝐿𝑗, respectively; 𝑥, 𝑧are pure-𝐻 kernel coefficients, 𝑄 = 𝑈𝑗 + 1, and 𝑎 = 𝐴𝑗 + 1.
8.2 The first ROOT / 𝑅𝑗 fitting point intrinsic to CENTRAL
The 𝑅𝑘-face of 𝑊 is genuinely
𝑊 = (𝛿 − 1)𝑛𝑖 + (𝑔 − 1)𝑛𝑗 + (𝜌𝑘 + 𝑇 − 1)𝑛𝑘. (W-K)
From ROOT and 𝑅𝑗,
𝐹 = (𝑛 − 1)𝑚 + (𝛿 − 1 + 𝑛𝑑 − 𝜁𝑎𝑖)𝑛𝑖
+ (𝑔 − 1 + 𝜁𝜌𝑗 − 𝑛𝑆)𝑛𝑗
+ (𝜌𝑘 + 𝑇 − 1 − 𝑛𝐶 − 𝜁𝑏𝑘)𝑛𝑘.
(HRJ)
This is a completed family. We do not claim a new exhaustive classification of the entire integral family
below; instead, we select one first point with the required properties.
For 𝜁 ≥ 1, set
𝑛𝜁 = ⌈ 𝜁𝑎𝑖 − 𝛿 + 1
𝑑 ⌉ ,
𝐼𝜁 = 𝛿 − 1 + 𝑛𝜁𝑑 − 𝜁𝑎𝑖, 𝐽 𝜁 = 𝑔 − 1 + 𝜁𝜌𝑗 − 𝑛𝜁𝑆.
Since 𝑎𝑖 > 𝑑, the sequence 𝑛𝜁 is strictly increasing and 0 ≤ 𝐼 𝜁 ≤ 𝑑 − 1. Moreover, 𝑛1 = ⌈(𝜆 + 1)/𝑑⌉ ≥ 2.
Because 𝑉 = 𝑑𝜌 𝑗 − 𝑆𝑎𝑖 > 0,
𝐽𝑑 = 𝑉 + 𝑆 ⌊ 𝛿 − 1
𝑑 ⌋ + 𝑔 − 1 > 0.
Hence
̂𝜁 = min{𝜁 ≥ 1 ∶ 𝐽𝜁 ≥ 0} ≤ 𝑑
exists. For brevity, write
𝑁 = 𝑛 ̂𝜁, 𝐼 = 𝐼 ̂𝜁, 𝐽 = 𝐽 ̂𝜁
If ̂𝜁 > 1, the preceding value of 𝐽 is negative and 𝑛 increases by at least one, so
𝐽 ≤ 𝜌 𝑗 − 𝑆 − 1.
A uniform safe cap, including ̂𝜁 = 1, is therefore
𝑁 ≥ 2, 0 ≤ 𝐼 ≤ 𝑑 − 1, 0 ≤ 𝐽 ≤ 𝜌 𝑗 − 𝑆 + 𝑔 − 1. (FIRST-CAPS)
Define
Ξ = 𝑁 𝐶 + ̂𝜁𝑏𝑘 − (𝜌𝑘 + 𝑇 − 1).
If Ξ ≤ 0, then HRJ itself is a nonnegative 𝐹-row, and the case is closed. Thus every survivor has Ξ ≥ 1.
̂Ω = 𝐹 + Ξ𝑛𝑘 = (𝑁 − 1)𝑚 + 𝐼𝑛𝑖 + 𝐽 𝑛𝑗
is a genuine ZERO-𝑘 face of the named actual upper element.

8.3 SAME-element packet and the FI/FJ level wall
8.3.1 Re-deriving CENTRAL FK
From 𝑊, ROOT, and 𝑅𝑗, exactly
𝐹 + 𝑛𝑘 = (𝑑 + 𝛽 − 1)𝑛𝑖 + (𝑅 + 𝜌𝑗 − 𝑆 − 1)𝑛𝑗 + (𝛼 − 𝐶)𝑛𝑘. (C-FK)
When 𝐶 > 𝛼, this expression cannot itself be called actual. However,
𝐹 + (𝐶 − 𝛼 + 1)𝑛𝑘 = (𝑑 + 𝛽 − 1)𝑛𝑖 + (𝑅 + 𝜌𝑗 − 𝑆 − 1)𝑛𝑗
is genuine.
Define
𝐷𝐹 = 𝑑 + 𝛽 − 1 − 𝐼 ≥ 𝛽 > 0,
𝐸𝐹 = 𝑅 + 𝜌𝑗 − 𝑆 − 1 − 𝐽 ≥ 𝑎 𝑗 > 0,
𝜒 = 𝐶 − 𝛼 + 1 − Ξ.
Raise the two actual upper elements to the same element using only additional copies of 𝑛𝑘, and then
remove their common 𝐼𝑛𝑖 + 𝐽 𝑛𝑗 coefficientwise. This gives
(𝑁 − 1)𝑚 +max(𝜒, 0)𝑛𝑘 = 𝐷𝐹 𝑛𝑖 + 𝐸𝐹 𝑛𝑗 + max(−𝜒, 0)𝑛𝑘. (PACKET)
Both sides of this displayed relation have nonnegative coefficients. In particular, we are not misrepre-
senting the signed FK relation as an actual factorization.
8.3.2 The strong wall at 𝐶 = 𝛼
At this boundary, 𝜒 = 1 − Ξ ≤ 0. Hence
(𝑁 − 1)𝑚 = 𝐷𝐹 𝑛𝑖 + 𝐸𝐹 𝑛𝑗 + (Ξ − 1)𝑛𝑘.
If 𝑁 − 1 ≤ 𝐿𝑖, we insert this packet into the 𝑚-part of the SAME EA representation, producing a positive
𝑖-coefficient and hence 𝑞𝐴𝑖
∈ Γ. The same applies to EB. If 𝑁 − 1 ≤ 𝐿 𝑗, we insert it into the SAME 𝑄𝑗
representation and produce a positive 𝑗-coefficient.
Therefore
𝐶 = 𝛼 ⟹ 𝑁 ≥ max(𝐿𝑖, 𝐿𝑏, 𝐿𝑗) + 2. (BOUNDARY -W ALL)
8.3.3 The wall for general 𝜒 ≤ 𝑇
Assume 𝜒 ≤ 𝑇 . If 𝑁 ≤ 𝐿 𝑖, apply PACKET inside the SAME FI-A representation and finally remove one
copy of 𝑛𝑖:
𝐹 = (𝐿𝑖 − 𝑁 )𝑚 + (𝐷𝐹 − 1)𝑛𝑖 + (𝑈𝑗 + 𝑔 + 𝐸𝐹 )𝑛𝑗 + (𝑈𝑘 + 𝑇 − 𝜒)𝑛𝑘 ∈ Γ. (FI-CERT)
If 𝑁 ≤ 𝐿𝑗, apply it inside the SAME FJ representation:
𝐹 = (𝐿𝑗 − 𝑁 )𝑚 + (𝐴𝑗 + 𝛿 + 𝐷𝐹 )𝑛𝑖 + (𝐸𝐹 − 1)𝑛𝑗 + (𝐶𝑗 + 𝑇 − 𝜒)𝑛𝑘 ∈ Γ. (FJ-CERT)

When 𝜒 > 0, the 𝑇 copies of 𝑛𝑘 in FI/FJ supply the required source. When 𝜒 ≤ 0, no 𝑛𝑘 is consumed and
copies are instead produced. Every coefficient in the final displayed representations is nonnegative.
Consequently,
𝜒 ≤ 𝑇 ⟹ 𝑁 ≥ max(𝐿𝑖, 𝐿𝑗) + 1. (WINDOW-W ALL)
We do not apply this 𝑇-window unconditionally to a general EB representation. The canonical 𝑘-

8.4 The pure- 𝐻 integer basis and two coefficients equal to one
8.4.1 Kernel basis
Use the integer kernel basis r𝑗 = (𝑎𝑖, −𝜌𝑗, 𝑏𝑘),r𝑘 = (𝑏𝑖, 𝑎𝑗, −𝜌𝑘)from §4.3. In all coefficient comparisons
below, the 𝑚-coordinate is eliminated completely before the comparison is made.
8.4.2 EA
We work under WINDOW-W ALL. Put𝐿 = 𝐿𝑖, 𝑀 = 𝐿𝑗, 𝑎 = 𝐴𝑗 + 1 ≥ 1, and 𝑄 = 𝑈𝑗 + 1 ≥ 1.
Compare the canonical and actual EA rows and eliminate 𝑚 completely using ROOT:
(𝑃 + 𝐿𝑑, 𝑎𝑗 − 1 − 𝐿𝑆 − 𝑈𝑗, −𝐿𝐶 − 𝑈𝑘 − 1) = 𝑥r𝑗 + 𝑦r𝑘.
The quantities 𝑥, 𝑦are integers. The first coordinate is positive, the third is negative, and the second is less
than 𝑎𝑗. If 𝑦 ≤ 0, the sign of the third coordinate forces 𝑥 ≤ 0, contradicting the first coordinate. If 𝑥 ≤ 0,
the second coordinate is at least 𝑎𝑗. Thus 𝑥, 𝑦 ≥ 1.
Comparing coefficients gives
𝐿𝑑 = 𝑥𝑎𝑖 + (𝑦 − 1)𝑏𝑖 − 𝛿, 𝐿𝑆 = 𝑥𝜌 𝑗 − (𝑦 − 1)𝑎𝑗 − 𝑄.
If 𝑦 ≥ 2, then at the HRJ point (𝜁, 𝑛) = (𝑥, 𝐿),
𝐼 = (𝑦 − 1)𝑏𝑖 − 1 ≥ 0, 𝐽 = 𝑄 + 𝑔 − 1 + (𝑦 − 1)𝑎𝑗 ≥ 0.
For the same 𝜁 = 𝑥 , minimizing 𝑛 only increases 𝐽, and 𝑛𝑥 ≤ 𝐿. Hence the first point also has 𝑁 ≤ 𝐿 ,
contradicting WINDOW-W ALL.
Therefore 𝑦 = 1:
𝐿𝑑 = 𝑥𝑎𝑖 − 𝛿, 𝐿𝑆 = 𝑥𝜌 𝑗 − 𝑄, 𝜀 ∶= 𝜌 𝑘 − 𝑈𝑘 − 1 = 𝐿𝐶 + 𝑥𝑏𝑘. (EA-ONE)
8.4.3 SAME Qj
After eliminating 𝑚 completely using ROOT,
(𝑀 𝑑 + 𝑏𝑖 − 1 − 𝐴𝑗, 𝑅 − 𝑀 𝑆, −𝑀 𝐶 − 𝐶𝑗 − 1) = 𝑧r𝑗 + 𝑣r𝑘.
The first coordinate is positive because 𝐴𝑗 ≤ 𝑑 − 1 , the third is negative, and the second is less than 𝑎𝑗
because 𝑆 ≥ 𝑔 + 1. The same sign argument gives 𝑧, 𝑣 ≥ 1.
If 𝑣 ≥ 2, then at the point (𝑧, 𝑀 ),
𝐼 = 𝐴 𝑗 + 𝛿 + (𝑣 − 1)𝑏𝑖 ≥ 0, 𝐽 = (𝑣 − 1)𝑎𝑗 − 1 ≥ 0.
Thus 𝑁 ≤ 𝑀 , contradicting WINDOW-W ALL. Hence

𝑀 𝑑 = 𝑧𝑎𝑖 + 𝑎, 𝑀 𝑆 = 𝑧𝜌 𝑗 + 𝑔, 𝜌 𝑘 = 𝑀 𝐶 + 𝐶𝑗 + 1 + 𝑧𝑏𝑘. (QJ-ONE)
8.5 An empty integer triangle, determinant one, and the level exception
Consider the triangle in the (𝜁, 𝑛)-plane with vertices
𝑂 = (0, 0), 𝑈 = (𝑥, 𝐿), 𝑉0 = (𝑧, 𝑀 )
The values of the affine functions 𝐼, 𝐽 at its vertices are
Vertex I J
O 𝛿 − 1 𝑔 − 1
U −1 𝑄 + 𝑔 − 1
V0 𝐴𝑗 + 𝛿 −1
At every nonvertex integer point, 𝐼 and 𝐽 are integers strictly greater than −1, hence nonnegative. More-
over, 0 < 𝑛 ≤ max(𝐿, 𝑀 ). Such a point would yield a first fit with 𝑁 ≤ max(𝐿, 𝑀 ), contradicting
WINDOW-W ALL.
On the other hand,
(𝑥𝑀 − 𝑧𝐿)𝑑 = 𝑥𝑎 + 𝑧𝛿 > 0.
If the positive integer determinant were at least two, the fundamental parallelogram would contain a nonzero
integer point, and either that point or its reflection across 𝑈 + 𝑉0 would give a nonvertex integer point of the
triangle. Hence
𝑥𝑀 − 𝑧𝐿 = 1. (DET1)
It follows that
𝑑 = 𝑥𝑎 + 𝑧𝛿, 𝑆 = 𝑥𝑔 + 𝑧𝑄,
𝑎𝑖 = 𝐿𝑎 + 𝑀 𝛿, 𝜌𝑗 = 𝐿𝑔 + 𝑀 𝑄,
𝑏𝑖 = 𝐿𝑎 + (𝑀 − 1)𝛿 + 𝛽, 𝑉 = 𝑎𝑄 − 𝛿𝑔 > 0.
(PARAM)
From 𝑎𝑖 > 𝑑 and DET1, the level is exactly
[𝐿 ≥ 𝑥 + 1 ≥ 2, 𝑀 > 𝑧 ≥ 1] ∨ [𝐿 = 𝑥 = 1, 𝑀 = 𝑧 + 1]. (LEVEL-SPLIT)
Indeed, if 𝑥 ≥ 2, 𝐿 ≤ 𝑥, then 𝑥𝑀 = 𝑧𝐿 + 1 ≤ 𝑧𝑥 + 1 , so 𝑀 ≤ 𝑧 , and hence 𝑎𝑖 ≤ 𝑑. If 𝑥 = 1, 𝐿 = 1,
DET1 gives 𝑀 = 𝑧 + 1. All other cases fall in the first alternative.
The second alternative is the level-one exception 𝒰, which is passed to § 9.1.
8.6 The exact 𝑘-ceiling from FJ absorption
EA-ONE is
𝛿𝑛𝑖 + 𝜀𝑛𝑘 = 𝐿𝑚 + 𝑄𝑛𝑗, 𝜀 = 𝐿𝐶 + 𝑥𝑏 𝑘.
If 𝜀 ≤ 𝐶𝑗 + 𝑇, the left packet lies inside the SAME FJ representation, creating a positive 𝑗-coefficient and
making 𝐹 actual. Therefore
48

PAGE 50
Canonical reduction and type T ype bound under canonical reduction
𝐻0 ∶= 𝐿𝐶 + (𝑥 − 1)𝑏𝑘 − 𝛼 − 𝐶𝑗 − 1 ≥ 0. (H0)
Together with QJ-ONE, this yields
𝜌𝑘 = (𝑀 + 𝐿)𝐶 + (𝑧 + 𝑥 − 1)𝑏𝑘 − 𝛼 − 𝐻0. (K-CEILING)
Only on the boundary 𝐶 = 𝛼,
𝐻0 = (𝐿 − 1)𝛼 + (𝑥 − 1)𝑏𝑘 − 𝐶𝑗 − 1,
𝜌𝑘 = (𝑀 + 𝐿 − 1)𝛼 + (𝑧 + 𝑥 − 1)𝑏𝑘 − 𝐻0.
In the general formula, 𝐿𝐶 − 𝛼must not be replaced by (𝐿 − 1)𝐶.
8.7 𝐶 = 𝛼: synchronization of actual EA/EB representations
Only here do we use BOUNDARY -W ALL. After eliminating𝑚, the pure EB row is
(𝑃 + 𝐿𝑏𝑑, −1 − 𝐿𝑏𝑆 − 𝑉𝑗, 𝑏 𝑘 − 1 − 𝐿𝑏𝐶 − 𝑉𝑘) = 𝑥𝑏r𝑗 + 𝑦𝑏r𝑘.
The first coordinate is positive, the second is negative, and the third is less than 𝑏𝑘, so 𝑥𝑏, 𝑦𝑏 ≥ 1. If 𝑦𝑏 ≥ 2,
then (𝑥𝑏, 𝐿𝑏)has both 𝐼, 𝐽 ≥ 0 , hence 𝑁 ≤ 𝐿𝑏, contradicting BOUNDARY -W ALL.
Thus
𝐿𝑏𝑑 = 𝑥𝑏𝑎𝑖 − 𝛿, 𝐿 𝑏𝑆 = 𝑥𝑏𝜌𝑗 − (𝑉𝑗 + 𝑎𝑗 + 1).
The same triangle argument applied to EB and SAME Qj gives
𝑥𝑏𝑀 − 𝑧𝐿𝑏 = 1.
Subtracting the EA determinant identity and combining the difference with the 𝑖-equations gives
(𝑀 𝑑 − 𝑧𝑎𝑖)(𝑥𝑏 − 𝑥) = 𝑎(𝑥𝑏 − 𝑥) = 0.
Since 𝑎 ≥ 1,
𝐿𝑏 = 𝐿𝑖 = 𝐿, 𝑥 𝑏 = 𝑥, 𝑈 𝑗 = 𝑉 𝑗 + 𝑎𝑗, 𝑉 𝑘 = 𝑈𝑘 + 𝑏𝑘. (SYNC)
In particular,
𝑄 = 𝑈𝑗 + 1 ≥ 𝑎𝑗 + 1 ≥ 2.
This synchronization follows from nonnegative representations of the same original elements together with
the level bound above.
Moreover, the alternative 𝐿 = 𝑥 = 1 in LEVEL-SPLIT is impossible on the boundary, because then
𝐻0 = −𝐶𝑗 − 1 < 0. Hence
𝐿 ≥ 𝑥 + 1 ≥ 2, 𝑀 > 𝑧 ≥ 1.

8.8 Multiplicity contradiction throughout 𝐶 = 𝛼
Set the canonical cross products
ℐ = 𝜌 𝑗𝜌𝑘 − 𝑎𝑗𝑏𝑘, 𝒥 = 𝑎 𝑖𝜌𝑘 + 𝑏𝑖𝑏𝑘, 𝒦 = 𝑏 𝑖𝜌𝑗 + 𝑎𝑖𝑎𝑗
For some 𝜎 > 0 ,
(𝑛𝑖, 𝑛𝑗, 𝑛𝑘) = 𝜎(ℐ, 𝒥, 𝒦), 𝑚 = 𝜎̂ 𝑚, ̂ 𝑚 = −𝑑ℐ + 𝑆𝒥 + 𝐶𝒦.
We do not assume 𝜎 = 1 .
𝐷𝑗 = 𝑑𝑎𝑗 + 𝑆𝑏𝑖, 𝑃 0 = 𝑉 + 𝑎 𝑖 > 0,
𝐶𝐴 = 𝒦 − (𝑀 + 𝐿 − 1)𝑃0, 𝐶 𝐵 = 𝐷𝑗 − 𝑏𝑖 − (𝑧 + 𝑥 − 1)𝑃0.
The boundary form of K-CEILING gives exactly
̂ 𝑚 − 𝒥 = 𝐻0𝑃0 + 𝛼𝐶𝐴 + 𝑏𝑘𝐶𝐵. (BOUND-MULT)
The positivity decomposition is
𝐶𝐴 = 𝑎𝐴𝑎 + 𝛿𝐴𝛿 + 𝛽𝜌𝑗, 𝐶 𝐵 = 𝑎𝐵𝑎 + 𝛿𝐵𝛿 + 𝛽(𝑆 − 1),
𝐴𝑎 = (𝐿 − 1)(𝑀 − 1)(𝑄 − 2) + 𝐿2(𝑔 − 1) + 𝐿(𝑎𝑗 − 1)
+ (𝐿 − 2)𝑀 + 2,
𝐴𝛿 = 𝑀 (𝑀 − 1)(𝑄 − 2) + (𝐿𝑀 + 𝑀 − 1)(𝑔 − 1)
+ 𝑀 (𝑎𝑗 − 1) + 𝑀2 + 𝑀 − 1,
𝐵𝑎 = [𝑧(𝐿 − 1) − 𝑥 + 1] (𝑄 − 2) + 𝐿𝑥(𝑔 − 1) + 𝑥(𝑎𝑗 − 1)
+ 𝑧(𝐿 − 𝑥 − 1) + (𝑧 − 1)(𝑥 − 1) + 1,
𝐵𝛿 = (𝑥𝑀 + 𝑧 − 1)(𝑔 − 1) + 𝑧(𝑀 − 1)(𝑄 − 2)
+ 𝑧(𝑎𝑗 − 1) + 𝑧𝑀 .
(POS-COEFF)
Under 𝑄 ≥ 2 , 𝑔, 𝑎𝑗 ≥ 1 , 𝐿 ≥ 𝑥 + 1 , and 𝑀 > 𝑧 , every displayed summand is nonnegative and each
coefficient is positive.
In particular, 𝑧(𝐿−1)−𝑥+1 = 𝑥(𝑧−1)+1+𝑧(𝐿−𝑥−1) ≥ 1. Hence 𝐶𝐴, 𝐶𝐵 > 0. By BOUND-MULT,
𝑚 > 𝑛𝑗, contradicting multiplicity.
𝐶 = 𝛼 CLOSED.
This covers both Δ0 = 1 and Δ0 ≥ 2, and all minimal and strict return levels.
8.9 Strict packet-window theorem
8.9.1 Claim
𝐶 > 𝛼, 𝜒 ≤ 𝑇 , 𝐿 𝑖 ≥ 2 ⟹ ⊥. (STRICT-WINDOW)
By WINDOW-W ALL, the arguments of §8.4–§8.6 apply unchanged. Since 𝐿 = 𝐿𝑖 ≥ 2, the exceptional
alternative in LEVEL-SPLIT does not occur. Hence 𝐿 ≥ 𝑥 + 1and 𝑀 > 𝑧. We do not use SYNC here; only
𝑄 ≥ 1 is needed.
Put 𝛾 = 𝐶 − 𝛼 ≥ 1 . From the general K-CEILING identity,
̂ 𝑚 − 𝒥 = 𝐻0𝑃0 + 𝛼𝐶𝐴 + 𝛾(𝐶𝐴 − 𝑃0) + 𝑏𝑘𝐶𝐵. (STRICT-J)

8.9.2 The regular range 𝑄 ≥ 2
The inequalities 𝐶𝐴, 𝐶𝐵 > 0 from §8.8 remain valid.
The coefficient of 𝑎 in 𝐶𝐴 − 𝑃0 is
𝐸𝑎 = [(𝐿 − 1)(𝑀 − 1) − 1] (𝑄 − 2) + 𝐿2(𝑔 − 1)
+ 𝐿(𝑎𝑗 − 1) + (𝐿 − 2)𝑀 − 𝐿.
The coefficient of 𝛿 is
𝐸𝛿 = 𝑀 (𝑀 − 1)(𝑄 − 2) + (𝐿𝑀 + 𝑀 )(𝑔 − 1) + 𝑀 (𝑎𝑗 − 1) + 𝑀2 > 0,
and the coefficient of 𝛽 is 𝜌𝑗 > 0.
The coefficient 𝐸𝑎 can be negative only in exactly the following three cases.
1. 𝐿 = 2, 𝑥 = 1, 𝑀 = 2𝑧 + 1, 𝑄 = 2, 𝑔 = 𝑎𝑗 = 1, 𝑧 ≥ 1.
2. 𝐿 = 2, 𝑥 = 1, 𝑧 = 1, 𝑀 = 3, 𝑄 = 3, 𝑔 = 𝑎𝑗 = 1.
3. 𝐿 = 3, 𝑥 = 2, 𝑧 = 1, 𝑀 = 2, 𝑄 = 2, 𝑔 = 𝑎𝑗 = 1.
For completeness: if 𝐿 ≥ 4 , then (𝐿 − 2)𝑀 − 𝐿 ≥ 𝐿 − 4 ≥ 0. If 𝐿 = 3, 𝑀 ≥ 3 , this term is again
nonnegative. If 𝐿 = 3, 𝑀 = 2, the determinant and level conditions force 𝑥 = 2, 𝑧 = 1, and 𝐸𝑎 = 𝑄 − 3 +
9(𝑔 − 1) + 3(𝑎𝑗 − 1). If 𝐿 = 2, then 𝑥 = 1, 𝑀 = 2𝑧 + 1, so 𝐸𝑎 = (2𝑧 − 1)(𝑄 − 2) + 4(𝑔 − 1) + 2(𝑎𝑗 − 1) − 2.
Exhausting the integral possibilities gives exactly the three cases above.
Outside these cases, 𝐶𝐴 − 𝑃0 > 0, and STRICT-J gives 𝑚 > 𝑛𝑗.
8.9.3 𝑄 = 1 and the three exceptions
We use a different positivity decomposition. Define
𝐴′ = 𝒦 − (𝑀 + 𝐿)𝑉 , 𝐵′ = 𝐷𝑗 − (𝑧 + 𝑥 − 1)𝑉 .
𝐴′ = 𝑎{𝐿2𝑔 + [(𝐿 − 1)𝑀 − 𝐿]𝑄 + 𝐿𝑎𝑗}
+ 𝛿{(𝑀 − 1)𝜌𝑗 + 𝑀 𝑎𝑗 + (𝑀 + 𝐿)𝑔} + 𝛽𝜌𝑗,
𝐵′ = 𝑎{𝑥𝑎𝑗 + 𝐿𝑥𝑔 + [𝑧(𝐿 − 1) − 𝑥 + 1]𝑄}
+ 𝛿{𝑧𝑎𝑗 + (𝑀 − 1)𝑆 + (𝑧 + 𝑥 − 1)𝑔} + 𝛽𝑆.
For 𝑄 ≥ 1, 𝐿 ≥ 𝑥 + 1, 𝑀 > 𝑧, we have 𝐴′, 𝐵′ > 0.
𝐷base = 2𝐴′ + 𝐵′ + 𝑉 − 𝒦.
Exactly,
̂ 𝑚 − 𝒦 = 𝐻0𝑉 + (𝛼 − 1)(𝑉 + 𝐴′) + (𝛾 − 1)𝐴′ + (𝑏𝑘 − 1)𝐵′ + 𝐷base. (STRICT-K)
The coefficient decomposition of 𝐷base is
𝐷base = 𝑎{(𝐿 + 𝑥)𝑎𝑗 + 𝐿(𝐿 + 𝑥)𝑔 + 𝐸𝑄}
+ 𝛿{(𝑀 − 1)𝜌𝑗 + (𝑀 + 𝑧)𝑎𝑗 + (𝑀 − 1)𝑆
+ [2(𝑀 + 𝐿) + 𝑧 + 𝑥 − 2]𝑔} + 𝛽(𝜌𝑗 + 𝑆),
𝐸 = (𝐿 − 2)𝑀 + (𝐿 − 1)𝑧 − 2𝐿 − 𝑥 + 2.

Canonical reduction and type T ype bound under canonical reduction
For 𝑄 = 1,
𝐸 − (𝐿 − 𝑥 − 3) = (𝐿 − 2)(𝑀 − 2) + (𝐿 − 1)(𝑧 − 1) ≥ 0,
so the coefficient of 𝑎 is at least (𝐿 + 𝑥)(𝐿 + 1) − 2, and is therefore positive. The other coefficients are
also positive.
In the three exceptional cases above, the coefficients of 𝑎 are respectively
2𝑧 + 3, 3, 16
all positive. Thus 𝐷base > 0 in those cases as well, and STRICT-K gives 𝑚 > 𝑛𝑘.
This exhausts the entire range 𝑄 ≥ 1 and proves STRICT-WINDOW .
The only finite branching used here is the set of three boundary cases derived from the sign calcula-
tion; no arbitrary numerical cutoff is used.
8.10 The closed strict infinite region and the exact source shortage
Every survivor has Ξ ≥ 1, so
𝜒 = 𝐶 − 𝛼 + 1 − Ξ ≤ 𝐶 − 𝛼.
Thus, if 𝐶 ≤ 𝑏 𝑘 + 2𝛼, then 𝜒 ≤ 𝑏 𝑘 + 𝛼 = 𝑇 . If in addition 𝜆 > 𝑑 , the intrinsic bound gives 𝐿𝑖 ≥ 2 .
Therefore
𝜆 > 𝑑, 𝛼 < 𝐶 ≤ 𝑏 𝑘 + 2𝛼 CLOSED.
In general, a survivor with 𝐿𝑖 ≥ 2 cannot lie in the window itself, so
𝜒 ≥ 𝑇 + 1 ⟺ 1 ≤ Ξ ≤ 𝐶 − 𝑏 𝑘 − 2𝛼. (DEEP)
In particular, 𝐶 ≥ 𝑏𝑘 + 2𝛼 + 1. This is precisely the shortage of 𝑘-source for the displayed packet.
8.11 The remaining level-one boundary inside the window
If a survivor satisfies 𝜒 ≤ 𝑇 , STRICT-WINDOW forces 𝐿𝑖 = 1. The intrinsic bound together with 𝑑 ≤ 𝜆
then gives 𝜆 = 𝑑 and 𝑞0 = 2. The wall, basis, and triangle arguments of § 8.4–§8.6 remain valid, so LEVEL-
SPLIT necessarily takes the form
𝐿 = 𝑥 = 1, 𝑀 = 𝑧 + 1.
Put 𝜂 = 𝐶 𝑗 + 1. Then PARAM becomes
𝑑 = 𝜆 = 𝑎 + 𝑧𝛿, 𝑆 = 𝑔 + 𝑧𝑄,
𝑎𝑖 = 𝑑 + 𝛿, 𝑏𝑖 = 𝑑 + 𝛽,
𝜌𝑗 = 𝑔 + (𝑧 + 1)𝑄, 𝐿𝑗 = 𝑧 + 1,
𝐴𝑗 = 𝑎 − 1, 𝜌𝑘 = (𝑧 + 1)𝐶 + 𝜂 + 𝑧𝑏𝑘,
𝑈𝑗 = 𝑄 − 1, 𝑈𝑘 = 𝑎𝑘 − 𝐶 − 1.
(UNIT-PARAM)
Also 𝐻0 equals 𝐶 − 𝛼 − 𝜂 ≥ 0.

8.11.1 The first point can be computed exactly
For 1 ≤ 𝜁 ≤ 𝑧 + 1 ,
𝑛𝜁 = 𝜁 + ⌈ (𝜁 − 1)𝛿 + 1
𝑑 ⌉ = 𝜁 + 1,
𝐽𝜁 = (𝜁 − 𝑧)𝑄 − 1.
Hence
̂𝜁 = 𝑧 + 1, 𝑁 = 𝑧 + 2, 𝐼 = 𝑎 − 1, 𝐽 = 𝑄 − 1,
Ξ = 𝐶 − 𝛼 − 𝜂 + 1, 𝜒 = 𝜂.
Thus
1 ≤ 𝜂 ≤ min(𝐶 − 𝛼, 𝑇 ). (UNIT-ETA)
The compact data are
Δ0 = (𝑧 − 1)𝑄 + 1, 𝜏 0 = 𝑄.
The case 𝑧 = 1 has Δ0 = 1, while 𝑧 ≥ 2 has Δ0 ≥ 2. Both are treated in § 9.1.
Notice that the first point of the window does not depend on the choice of EA representation. Hence, if a
survivor actually exists, the presence of any other EA factorization of level at least two would itself close
the case by STRICT-WINDOW. Thus, in this exceptional situation, every missing- 𝑖 EA factorization must
have level one. This is not an assumption of uniqueness; it is a necessary condition deduced by applying the
preceding theorem.
8.11.2 Excluding the unit boundary by multiplicity
𝐷𝑢 ∶= 𝑄𝜂 − 𝑅(𝐶 + 𝑏𝑘).
Substitute the unit parameters into the cross products. Set
𝑀𝑎 = 𝑅(𝐶 + 𝑏𝑘) − 𝑄𝜂 = −𝐷𝑢,
𝑀𝛿 = 𝐶[𝑄𝑧(𝑧 + 1) + 𝑎𝑗(𝑧 + 1) + 𝑔(2𝑧 + 1)]
+ 𝑏𝑘[𝑄𝑧2 + 𝑎𝑗𝑧 + 2𝑔𝑧] + 𝜂𝑔 > 0,
𝑀𝛽 = 𝐶[𝑄(𝑧 + 1) + 𝑔] + 𝑏𝑘[𝑄𝑧 + 𝑔] > 0.
Exactly,
̂ 𝑚 = 𝑎𝑀𝑎 + 𝛿𝑀𝛿 + 𝛽𝑀𝛽,
̂ 𝑚 − ℐ = (𝑎 + 𝑧 + 1)𝑀𝑎 + (𝛿 − 1)𝑀𝛿 + (𝛽 − 1)𝑀𝛽. (UNIT-MULT)
If 𝐷𝑢 ≤ 0, the right-hand side is nonnegative, giving 𝑚 ≥ 𝑛𝑖, contrary to strict multiplicity. Therefore
𝐷𝑢 > 0. (UNIT-REMAIN)
Since 𝜂 ≤ 𝐶 − 𝛼 < 𝐶 + 𝑏 𝑘, in particular

Canonical reduction and type T ype bound under canonical reduction
𝑄 > 𝑅 = 𝑎𝑗 + 𝑔.
Thus the entire unit-boundary range 𝑄 ≤ 𝑅 is excluded as well.
Moreover, 𝑈𝑗 = 𝑄 − 1 ≥ 𝑎 𝑗, so actual transport through the canonical EA/EB difference constructs, in
the same configuration, a level-one EB representation
𝐸𝐵 = 𝑚 + (𝑄 − 𝑎𝑗 − 1)𝑛𝑗 + (𝑈𝑘 + 𝑏𝑘)𝑛𝑘
This does not assert uniqueness of the level of an arbitrarily chosen EB representation.
8.12 Exhaustive handoff
The boundary 𝐶 = 𝛼 has been eliminated. In the strict case 𝐶 > 𝛼 , the conjunction 𝜒 ≤ 𝑇 and 𝐿𝑖 ≥ 2 is
impossible. If 𝜒 ≤ 𝑇 , we are in the 𝒰-parameter region of § 8.11. On the other hand, if 𝜒 > 𝑇 , then
𝒟 ∶ 𝑇 < 𝜒 ≤ 𝐶 − 𝛼, 1 ≤ Ξ ≤ 𝐶 − 𝑏 𝑘 − 2𝛼, 𝐶 ≥ 𝑏 𝑘 + 2𝛼 + 1.
Thus the remaining cases are exhausted by 𝒰 and 𝒟.

9 From the residual cases to two packet relations
We treat in turn the two regions passed from the preceding section. The region 𝒰 is eliminated by repeated
exchanges within the same upper element. In 𝒟, we reduce the first point to the linear case and use a 715-
term identity to secure the required 𝑖-source. The remaining shortage of 𝑗-source yields two genuine packets,
which are embedded into the abstract input of the next section.

9.2 Exact parameterization of 𝒰
𝑎 ∶= 𝐴𝑗 + 1 ≥ 1, 𝑧 ≥ 1, 𝑄 ∶= 𝑈 𝑗 + 1 ≥ 1.
𝜆 = 𝑑 = 𝑎 + 𝑧𝛿, 𝑆 = 𝑔 + 𝑧𝑄,
𝑎𝑖 = 𝑑 + 𝛿, 𝑏𝑖 = 𝑑 + 𝛽,
𝜌𝑗 = 𝑔 + (𝑧 + 1)𝑄, 𝜌𝑘 = (𝑧 + 1)𝐶 + 𝜂 + 𝑧𝑏𝑘.
(U-PARAM)
1 ≤ 𝜂 ≤ min(𝐶 − 𝛼, 𝑇 ), 𝐷 𝑢 ∶= 𝑄𝜂 − 𝑅(𝐶 + 𝑏𝑘) > 0. (U-SIGNS)

Here 𝜂 = 𝐶 𝑗 + 1 and 𝐿𝑗 = 𝑧 + 1. By § 8.11,

9.3 The required number of packets and the 𝑗-capacity
Set
𝐻𝑢 ∶= 𝐶 − 𝛼 − 𝜂 ≥ 0, 𝑏 0 ∶= 𝑧𝛿 + 𝛽 > 0.
Let 𝑡 be the number of repetitions:
𝑡 ∶= ⌊ 𝐻𝑢
𝜂 ⌋ + 1 ≥ 1, 𝐻 𝑢 = (𝑡 − 1)𝜂 + 𝑟, 0 ≤ 𝑟 ≤ 𝜂 − 1. (COUNT)
Thus
𝐶 = 𝛼 + 𝑡𝜂 + 𝑟.
Since 𝜂 ≤ 𝑇 = 𝑏 𝑘 + 𝛼,
𝐶 + 𝑏𝑘 = 𝑇 + 𝑡𝜂 + 𝑟 ≥ (𝑡 + 1)𝜂.
From 𝐷𝑢 > 0 and integrality,
𝑄 ≥ (𝑡 + 1)𝑅 + 1. (J-FIT)
This estimate is uniform over all parameters and imposes no a priori bound on the number of repetitions.
9.4 Multiplicity forces the required 𝑖-capacity
9.4.1 Claim
Every survivor in 𝒰 satisfies
𝑎 ≥ 𝑡(𝑧𝛿 + 𝛽) + 𝛽 + 1 (I-FIT)
9.4.2 Scope of the cross-product argument
Define
ℐ = 𝜌 𝑗𝜌𝑘 − 𝑎𝑗𝑏𝑘, 𝒥 = 𝑎 𝑖𝜌𝑘 + 𝑏𝑖𝑏𝑘, 𝒦 = 𝑏 𝑖𝜌𝑗 + 𝑎𝑖𝑎𝑗.
The canonical relations imply that, for a common 𝜎 > 0 ,
(𝑛𝑖, 𝑛𝑗, 𝑛𝑘) = 𝜎(ℐ, 𝒥, 𝒦).
We make no additional assumption that 𝜎 = 1 , nor any additional gcd assumption. From ROOT,
𝑚 = 𝜎̂ 𝑚, ̂ 𝑚 = −𝑑ℐ + 𝑆𝒥 + 𝐶𝒦.

Set

Under U-PARAM, Φ is linear in 𝑎, and exactly
𝜕Φ
𝜕𝑎 = −𝐷𝑢 − 𝜌𝑘 − 𝑏𝑘 < 0. (MONO-A)
9.4.3 Positivity at the threshold
𝑎∗ ∶= 𝑡(𝑧𝛿 + 𝛽) + 𝛽.
𝑤 ∶= 𝑇 − 𝜂 ≥ 0, 𝑞 ′ ∶= 𝑄 − (𝑡 + 1)(𝑎𝑗 + 𝑔) − 1 ≥ 0.
After substituting 𝐶 = 𝛼 + 𝑡𝜂 + 𝑟 and 𝑏𝑘 = 𝜂 − 𝛼 + 𝑤, exactly
Φ(𝑎∗) = 𝛿{𝑃𝛿 + (𝑎𝑗 − 1)𝑋𝛿 + (𝑔 − 1)𝑌𝛿 + 𝑞′𝑍𝛿}
+ 𝛽{𝑃𝛽 + (𝑎𝑗 − 1)𝑋𝛽 + (𝑔 − 1)𝑌𝛽 + 𝑞′𝑍𝛽}.
(POS-DECOMP)
The polynomials 𝑋, 𝑌 , 𝑍 are nonnegative and are displayed explicitly in Appendix C. The two leading
terms are
𝑃𝛿 = 𝛼(2𝑡𝑧 + 3𝑧 + 1)
+ 𝜂(𝑡2𝑧2 + 𝑡2𝑧 + 3𝑡𝑧2 + 𝑡𝑧 + 𝑡 + 2𝑧2)
+ 𝑟(𝑡𝑧2 + 3𝑡𝑧 + 2𝑧2 + 4𝑧 + 1)
+ 𝑤𝑧(𝑡𝑧 + 𝑡 + 2𝑧 + 1) > 0,
𝑃𝛽 = 2𝛼(𝑡 + 2)
+ 𝜂(𝑡2𝑧 + 𝑡2 + 3𝑡𝑧 + 2𝑧 − 3)
+ 𝑟(𝑡𝑧 + 3𝑡 + 2𝑧 + 5)
+ 𝑤(𝑡𝑧 + 𝑡 + 2𝑧 + 1) > 0.
The only bracket that appears to contain a negative contribution is nevertheless positive, since 𝑡, 𝑧 ≥ 1:
𝑡2𝑧 + 𝑡2 + 3𝑡𝑧 + 2𝑧 − 3 ≥ 4.
Hence Φ(𝑎∗) > 0. If 𝑎 ≤ 𝑎∗, MONO-A gives
Φ(𝑎) ≥ Φ(𝑎∗) > 0,
so 𝑚 > 𝑛𝑗, contradicting multiplicity. Integrality therefore yields I-FIT.
This comparison does not invoke criticality for a signed relation; it is a contradiction between an explicit
positive polynomial and strict multiplicity.
9.5 Replacing 𝑡 packets inside the SAME actual upper element and actualizing the PF gap
From U-PARAM,
𝑏0𝑛𝑖 + 𝑅𝑛𝑗 = (𝑧 + 1)𝑚 + 𝜂𝑛𝑘. (U-PACKET)
Both sides have nonnegative coefficients.
56

PAGE 58
Canonical reduction and type T ype bound under canonical reduction
For the same 𝑞𝐴𝑘
, the following identity holds:
𝑞𝐴𝑘
+ (𝐻𝑢 + 1)𝑛𝑘 = (𝑧 + 2)𝑚 + (𝑎 − 𝛽 − 1)𝑛𝑖 + (𝑄 − 𝑅 − 1)𝑛𝑗. (U-UPPER)
The left side is a named actual upper element because 𝑞𝐴𝑘
+ 𝑛𝑘 ∈ Γ and 𝐻𝑢 ≥ 0.
By I-FIT and J-FIT,
𝑎 − 𝛽 − 1 ≥ 𝑡𝑏0, 𝑄 − 𝑅 − 1 ≥ 𝑡𝑅.
Thus the right side of U-UPPER is also genuine and contains coefficientwise 𝑡 copies of the left side of
U-PACKET. Replace all of them by the right side of U-PACKET. The resulting 𝑘-coefficient in the same
upper element is 𝑡𝜂.
𝑡𝜂 − (𝐻𝑢 + 1) = 𝜂 − 𝑟 − 1 ≥ 0.
We can therefore remove the entire same added term (𝐻𝑢 + 1)𝑛𝑘, leaving
𝑞𝐴𝑘
= [𝑧 + 2 + 𝑡(𝑧 + 1)]𝑚
+ [𝑎 − 𝛽 − 1 − 𝑡(𝑧𝛿 + 𝛽)]𝑛𝑖
+ [𝑄 − (𝑡 + 1)𝑅 − 1]𝑛𝑗
+ (𝜂 − 𝑟 − 1)𝑛𝑘 ∈ Γ.
(U-FINAL)
All four coefficients are nonnegative. This contradicts 𝑞𝐴𝑘
∈ 𝑃 𝐹 (Γ).
𝒰 = ∅.
No intermediate signed row has been treated as actual, and no second factorization of𝑄𝑘 has been assumed.
The required number 𝑡 is determined by the exact floor above.

9.7 The last crossing is an ℎ-step
Since 𝑎𝑖 = ℎ𝑑+𝑏with 0 < 𝑏 < 𝑑 , successive increments of𝑛are either ℎor ℎ+1. At 𝑧 = 1, 𝑛1 = ℎ+1 = 𝑞0,
and
𝐽1 = 𝜌𝑗 + 𝑔 − 1 − 𝑞0𝑆 = −Δ0 < 0.
Hence 𝑧 ≥ 2. If the final increment were ℎ + 1, then
𝐽𝑧 − 𝐽𝑧−1 = 𝜌𝑗 − (ℎ + 1)𝑆 = 𝜏 − 𝑆 ≤ −𝑔 < 0,
so the sequence could not cross from negative to nonnegative. Therefore
𝑛𝑧 − 𝑛𝑧−1 = ℎ.
Consequently,
0 ≤ 𝐽 ≤ 𝜏 − 1, 0 ≤ 𝐼 ≤ 𝑑 − 𝑏 − 1 = 𝑒 0 − 𝛿 − 1. (FIRST-CAPS)
The upper bound for 𝐼 follows from 𝐼𝑧−1 = 𝐼 + 𝑏 ≤ 𝑑 − 1 .
We use the following two exact identities:
𝑁 𝑉 = 𝜌 𝑗(𝐼 − 𝛿 + 1) + 𝑎𝑖(𝐽 − 𝑔 + 1),
𝑧𝑉 = 𝑆(𝐼 − 𝛿 + 1) + 𝑑(𝐽 − 𝑔 + 1). (NV-ZV)
FIRST-CAPS gives
𝑁 𝑉 ≤ ℎ𝑉 + (𝑑 − 𝛿)𝜌𝑗 − 𝑎𝑖𝑔,
(𝑧 − 1)𝑉 ≤ 𝑆(𝑑 − 𝛿) − 𝑑𝑔. (FIRST-BOUNDS)
If ℎ ≥ 2, then
𝐸𝐹 ≥ 𝑅 + (ℎ − 1)𝑆 > 𝜏 − 1 ≥ 𝐽 .
Since 𝑅 + 𝜌𝑗 − 𝑆 − 1 = 𝐸𝐹 + 𝐽, two or more packets cannot fit into the 𝑗-source of Ω𝐷. For this high- ℎ
range, we instead use the multiplicity comparison below.
9.8 A common multiplicity certificate

(𝑛𝑖, 𝑛𝑗, 𝑛𝑘) = 𝜎(ℐ, 𝒥, 𝒦), 𝑚 = 𝜎̂ 𝑚,

9.9 Excluding 𝒟 when 𝑞0 ≥ 3
Assume ℎ ≥ 2. FIRST-BOUNDS gives
𝐴 ≥ 𝐴0 ∶= 𝒦 − ℎ𝑉 − (𝑑 − 𝛿)𝜌𝑗 + 𝑎𝑖𝑔,
𝐵 ≥ 𝐵0 ∶= 𝑑𝑅 + 𝑆(𝑃 − 𝑑) > 0.
Write
𝐴0 = 𝑆𝐴𝑆 + 𝜏 𝐴𝜏 + 𝑎𝑖𝑅,
where
𝐴𝑆 = ℎ[(ℎ − 1)𝑑 + 𝛽 + 2𝛿 + 2𝑟], 𝐴𝜏 = 𝛽 − 𝑑 + 𝛿 + 𝑟.
Moreover,
𝐴𝑆 + 𝐴𝜏 = [ℎ(ℎ − 1) − 1]𝑑 + (ℎ + 1)𝛽 + (2ℎ + 1)(𝛿 + 𝑟) > 0.
Since 𝑆 > 𝜏 > 0 , it follows that 𝐴0 > 0.
A direct expansion gives
4𝐴0 + 𝐵0 + 𝑉 − 𝒦 = 𝑆𝐻 𝑆 + 𝜏 𝐻𝜏 + 𝐻𝑐,
where
𝐻𝑆 = (3ℎ2 − 3ℎ − 1)𝑑 + (3ℎ + 1)𝛽 + 8ℎ𝛿 + 7ℎ𝑟 > 0,
𝐻𝜏 = 3𝛽 − (ℎ + 3)𝑑 + 4𝛿 + 3𝑟,
𝐻𝑐 = 𝑎𝑗[(3ℎ + 1)𝑑 + 3(𝛿 + 𝑟)] + 𝑔[(4ℎ + 1)𝑑 + 4(𝛿 + 𝑟)] > 0.
60

PAGE 62
Canonical reduction and type T ype bound under canonical reduction
The sum of the first two coefficients is
𝐻𝑆 + 𝐻𝜏 = (ℎ − 2)(3ℎ + 2)𝑑 + (3ℎ + 4)𝛽 + (8ℎ + 4)𝛿 + (7ℎ + 3)𝑟 > 0.
Thus
𝑆𝐻𝑆 + 𝜏 𝐻𝜏 = (𝑆 − 𝜏 )𝐻𝑆 + 𝜏 (𝐻𝑆 + 𝐻𝜏 ) > 0.
M-MASTER then yields ̂ 𝑚 > 𝒦, i.e. 𝑚 > 𝑛𝑘, a contradiction.
𝒟 ∩ {𝑞0 ≥ 3} = ∅. (HIGH-Q-CLOSED)
Hence the only possible cases in 𝒟 have ℎ = 1, equivalently 𝑑 ≤ 𝜆 < 2𝑑.
9.10 When 𝑞0 = 2, the first point satisfies 𝑁 = 𝑧 + 1
Write 𝜆 = 𝑑 + 𝑟, 𝑎𝑖 = 𝑑 + 𝑏, and 𝑏 = 𝑟 + 𝛿. Then
𝑛𝑧 = 𝑧 + ℓ 𝑧, ℓ 𝑧 = ⌈ 𝑧𝑏 − 𝛿 + 1
𝑑 ⌉ ≥ 1.
We show that no first point in 𝒟 can have ℓ𝑧 ≥ 2.
Suppose otherwise and set
𝜅 = ⌊ 𝑑 + 𝛿 − 1
𝑏 ⌋ ≥ 1.
For 1 ≤ 𝑣 ≤ 𝜅, the candidate points have ℓ𝑣 = 1. None is the first successful point, so
𝑆 ≥ 𝜅𝜏 + 𝑔.
Put 𝑟′ = 𝑑 − 𝜅𝑏. The slope identity
𝑉 = 𝑑𝜏 − 𝑏𝑆 ≤ 𝑟 ′𝜏 − 𝑏𝑔 > 0
implies 𝑟′ > 0. From the definition of the floor,
1 ≤ 𝑟′ ≤ 𝑏 − 𝛿. (SMALL-R)
If the first point had 𝐼 ≥ 𝑟 ′, then the preceding integer point
(𝑧 − 𝜅, ℓ𝑧 − 1)
would have𝑖-coefficient 𝐼 −𝑟′ ≥ 0 and 𝑗-coefficient 𝐽 +𝑆−𝜅𝜏 ≥ 𝑔 > 0. Its 𝑧-index is positive and smaller
than the original 𝑧. Replacing 𝑛 by the least allowed value only increases the 𝑗-coefficient, contradicting the
definition of the first point. Therefore
0 ≤ 𝐼 ≤ 𝑟 ′ − 1.

𝒟 > 0. (DSCR)

9.12.2 Proof
In the parameterization of § 9.11, hold the remaining parameters fixed and set Φ(𝑎) = ̂ 𝑚 − 𝒥. Direct differ-
entiation gives
Φ′(𝑎) = −𝒟 − 𝜌𝑘 − 𝑏𝑘 < 0.
Consider the threshold 𝑎∗ = 𝑓𝐵 + 𝑟. Substitute
𝑄 = 𝑓(𝑎𝑗 + 𝑔 + 𝜐) + 𝜃, 𝐶 = 𝛼 + 𝑓𝜒 + 𝑤, 𝜒 = 𝑏 𝑘 + 𝛼 + 1 + 𝜖, 𝜖 ≥ 0,
𝑏 = 𝑟 + 𝛿, 𝑐 = 𝑟 + 𝛽, 𝐺 = 𝑔 + 𝜐.

10 Euclidean descent and source containment
This section uses only the abstract inputs listed below. The minimality of the first point constructed in the
preceding section is not required in the descent that follows. At a terminal state we show that the required
source is contained in the same 𝑊; at a nonterminal state we construct a transformation preserving every
input condition and strictly decreasing a positive integer measure.
10.1 Euclidean two-packet closure theorem
We introduce new local packet notation here. The indices 𝑁 , 𝐼, 𝐽attached to the first point in § 9.6 are not
carried into this theorem. The common parameters 𝛿, 𝛽, 𝑔, 𝛼, 𝑎𝑗, 𝑏𝑘 are positive integers, 𝑃 is a positive
integer, 𝑎𝑖 = 𝑃 − 𝛽 > 0, 𝑏 𝑖 = 𝑃 − 𝛿 > 0 , and 𝑅 = 𝑎 𝑗 + 𝑔, 𝑇 = 𝑏 𝑘 + 𝛼. We retain the original HCR and
𝑊 = (𝑃 − 1)𝑛𝑖 + (𝑅 − 1)𝑛𝑗 + (𝑇 − 1)𝑛𝑘 = 𝐹 + 𝑚. We denote the packet coefficient by 𝐻𝑝, its determinant
by 𝒟𝑝, and the descent measure by ℳ. The variables 𝑢, 𝜃, 𝑤, 𝜒are integers with 𝑢 ≥ 1, 𝑤 ≥ 0, and 𝜒 ≥ 1.
10.2 Definition
For the same 𝑚, 𝑛𝑖, 𝑛𝑗, 𝑛𝑘, 𝐹 , 𝑊, assume the following.
M = (𝑝 𝑞
𝑠 𝑡 ) , 𝑝, 𝑞, 𝑠, 𝑡 ∈ ℤ ≥1, 𝑝𝑡 − 𝑞𝑠 = 1,
𝐿 = 𝑝 + 𝑞 > 𝑀 = 𝑠 + 𝑡. (MATRIX)
𝑟 ∈ ℤ ≥0, 𝐴 = 𝑝(𝑟 + 𝛿) + 𝑞(𝑟 + 𝛽), 𝐵 = 𝑠(𝑟 + 𝛿) + 𝑡(𝑟 + 𝛽). (SOURCES)
𝐸 = 𝑅 + 𝑢, 𝐻 𝑝 = 𝑇 + 𝑤, 1 ≤ 𝜃 ≤ 𝑢, 𝑤 ≥ 0, 𝜒 ≥ 1,
𝒟𝑝 ∶= 𝜃𝜒 − 𝐸𝐻𝑝 > 0. (DET-POS)
The two genuine equalities are
𝐵𝑛𝑖 + 𝐸𝑛𝑗 = 𝑀 𝑚 + 𝜒𝑛𝑘, (DP)
𝐴𝑛𝑖 + 𝐻𝑝𝑛𝑘 = 𝐿𝑚 + 𝜃𝑛𝑗. (PP)
For the canonical multipliers we require
𝜌𝑗 = 𝐿𝐸 + 𝑀 𝜃 − 𝑎𝑗, 𝜌 𝑘 = 𝐿𝜒 + 𝑀 𝐻𝑝 − 𝑏𝑘. (RHO)
The package also includes 𝑎𝑖 = 𝑃 − 𝛽 > 0 , 𝑏𝑖 = 𝑃 − 𝛿 > 0 , the relations (HCR), and the original
representation of 𝑊.
10.3 Theorem
Euclidean two-packet closure theorem.
If this package holds together with 𝑚 < min(𝑛𝑖, 𝑛𝑗, 𝑛𝑘), then
𝐹 ∈ Γ.
Consequently, no package of this form can occur when 𝐹 is a gap.
We prove the theorem by strong induction on the positive integer

The quantity ℳ is the descent measure used only in the present argument; it is not a depth parameter
inherited from an earlier branch.

Set
𝜈 = ⌊ 𝑢

𝑍𝑛𝑖 + 𝑗∗𝑛𝑗 + 𝑘∗𝑛𝑘 = 𝑁 𝑚, (TERMINAL-PACKET)
where

Always,
𝑗∗ ≤ 𝑅 − 1.

𝑘∗ ≤ 𝑇 − 1. (TERMINAL)
If it holds, then the inequality 𝑃 ≥ 𝑍 + 1 , proved in the next subsection, gives
𝐹 = (𝑁 − 1)𝑚 + (𝑃 − 1 − 𝑍)𝑛𝑖 + (𝑅 − 1 − 𝑗∗)𝑛𝑗 + (𝑇 − 1 − 𝑘∗)𝑛𝑘 ∈ Γ. (FINAL-F)
10.4.1 Actual replacement within the same 𝑊
The quantities 𝑗∗ and 𝑘∗ may be negative. In that event, rewrite (TERMINAL-PACKET) as
𝑍𝑛𝑖 + (𝑗∗)+𝑛𝑗 + (𝑘∗)+𝑛𝑘 = 𝑁 𝑚 + (−𝑗∗)+𝑛𝑗 + (−𝑘∗)+𝑛𝑘
Every coefficient on both sides is nonnegative.
The existing face of the SAME 𝑊 contains the left source coefficientwise. Replacing that source by the
right side produces a new genuine factorization of 𝑊. Since 𝑁 ≥ 1, we then remove one copy of 𝑚from the
final vector to obtain (FINAL-F). No external packet has been added, and no shift remains to be removed.

10.6 Positivity order of the matrix
From (MATRIX),
𝑝 ≥ 𝑠 + 1, 𝑞 ≥ 𝑡. (ORDER)
Indeed, if 𝑝 ≤ 𝑠 , positivity of the determinant forces 𝑞 < 𝑡 , hence 𝐿 < 𝑀 . Thus 𝑝 > 𝑠 . If next 𝑞 < 𝑡 ,
write 𝑝 = 𝑠 + 𝑎, 𝑡 = 𝑞 + 𝑏 with 𝑎, 𝑏 ≥ 1. Then

ℐ = 𝜌 𝑗𝜌𝑘 − 𝑎𝑗𝑏𝑘, 𝒥 = 𝑎 𝑖𝜌𝑘 + 𝑏𝑖𝑏𝑘, 𝒦 = 𝑏 𝑖𝜌𝑗 + 𝑎𝑖𝑎𝑗.

10.15 Strict descent
The new measure is

ℳ − ℳ′ = (𝑒 − 1)(𝐻𝑝 + 𝜃) + 𝜌 + 𝜅 > 0. (DESCENT)
The positive integer measure therefore decreases by at least one at every step, so failure cannot continue
indefinitely. Strong induction shows that every abstract state reaches a terminal state after finitely many

color exchange only renames generators, this is the assertion 𝐹 ∈ Γ in the original Γ.
This proves the theorem § 10.1.

11 Proof of the main theorem
We now combine the conclusion for the symmetric tail, the classification of the nonsymmetric tail into three
terminal configurations, and the elimination of each configuration. Starting from the contradiction hypothesis
obtained by selecting four rows, we return to the cardinality bound for the full set 𝑄(Γ).
11.1 Completion of the CHAIN closure
§7.1 supplies the CORE of § 7.7 from every actual CHAIN configuration. In § 8.1, the boundary 𝐶 = 𝛼 is
eliminated and the strict case is divided into 𝒰 ∨ 𝒟. The region 𝒰 is eliminated in §9.1. In § 9.6, 𝒟 is reduced
to 𝑞0 = 2 and 𝑁 = ̂𝜁 + 1; if 𝜃 ≥ 𝜐 + 1, a nonnegative representation of the same 𝐹 gives a contradiction. In
the remaining range 1 ≤ 𝜃 ≤ 𝜐 , the determinant-one matrix of § 9.15 satisfies every hypothesis of §10.1. The
termination and terminal source-fit in § 10.1 then produce a nonnegative representation of 𝐹 in the original
Γ. Hence CHAIN cannot occur.
11.2 Proof of the main theorem § 1.1
Let Γ be a minimally four-generated numerical semigroup satisfying the canonical condition of Sections 1–2,
and suppose that |𝑄(Γ)| ≥ 4.
By § 2.6, for every 𝑞 ∈ 𝑄(Γ)we have 𝑞 + 𝑚, 𝑐𝑞, 𝑊 ∈ Ap(Γ, 𝑚) ⊆ 𝐻, 𝑐𝑞 − 𝑚 ∉ Γ , and KEY holds.
If four distinct rows are selected, the same Γ, 𝐹 , 𝑚, 𝑊, together with their actuality, pseudo-Frobenius, and
complement conditions, are preserved.
We first exclude the third possibility for the tail gcd. Choose any 𝑞 ∈ 𝑄(Γ). Then
𝑚 = (𝑞 + 𝑚) + (𝑊 − 𝑞) − 𝑊 ∈ ℤ𝐻.
Hence gcd(𝑛1, 𝑛2, 𝑛3)also divides 𝑚. Since gcd (𝑚, 𝑛1, 𝑛2, 𝑛3) = 1, the tail gcd is one. Moreover, mini-
mality of Γimplies that the three generators of the tail are themselves minimal. Thus𝐻 is a genuine minimally
73

PAGE 75
Canonical reduction and type T ype bound under canonical reduction
three-generated numerical semigroup, and is either symmetric or nonsymmetric. The signed identity above
need not be called a nonnegative factorization.
If 𝐻 is symmetric, § 3.1 eliminates the contradiction hypothesis. If 𝐻 is nonsymmetric, then G4, § 4.6,
and Appendix B show that the surviving configuration of the four selected rows is one of PATH, TYPE II, or
CHAIN. The case of three singletons, in which the full set 𝑄 consists of only three rows, is proved separately
in § 4.13. Four-row configurations containing a corner are excluded by COLOR-CAP , and a matched pair
together with a singleton is excluded in § 4.10.2. This classification does not assume that the full set 𝑄 has
exactly four elements.
PATH is impossible by § 5.9, and TYPE II by § 6.1. A CHAIN configuration has, by § 7.1, the CORE-
ROOT structure of the same configuration and passes through § 7.7–§9.6 into §10.1. The full color exchange
of §10.1 preserves the original Γ, 𝐹 , 𝑚, 𝑊, and every nonterminal step strictly decreases the positive integer
ℳ = 𝐸 + 𝜒. After finitely many steps a terminal state is reached, and replacing the packet coefficientwise
inside the source of that same 𝑊 yields a factorization of the original 𝐹 with all coefficients nonnegative.
This contradicts 𝐹 ∉ Γ .
Thus every case arising from the contradiction hypothesis is impossible, and therefore
|𝑄(Γ)| ≤ 3.
Since 𝐹 ∈ 𝑃 𝐹 (Γ),
𝑡(Γ) = |𝑄(Γ)| + 1 ≤ 4.
This proves Theorem §1.1 from Sections 1–2.

PAGE 76
Canonical reduction and type T ype bound under canonical reduction
A Auxiliary calculations and complete branching for the symmetric tail
A.1 Elementary two-generator facts
Let 𝑇 = ⟨𝑢, 𝑣⟩, with gcd (𝑢, 𝑣) = 1and 𝑢, 𝑣 ≥ 2. Every integer has a unique representation
𝑎𝑢 + 𝑏𝑣, 𝑎 ∈ ℤ, 0 ≤ 𝑏 < 𝑢
and membership in 𝑇 is equivalent to 𝑎 ≥ 0 . The difference between any two integral coefficient repre-
sentations is (𝑘𝑣, −𝑘𝑢). The following consequences will be used.
• 𝐹𝑇 = 𝑢𝑣 − 𝑢 − 𝑣 , and for every integer 𝑡, 𝑡 ∉ 𝑇 ⟺ 𝐹 𝑇 − 𝑡 ∈ 𝑇 . Indeed, the complementary
representation to the normal form above is (−𝑎 − 1)𝑢 + (𝑢 − 𝑏 − 1)𝑣.
• If 𝑃 < 𝑣, 𝑄 < 𝑢 and 𝑃 𝑢 + 𝑄𝑣 ∈ 𝑇 , then 𝑃 , 𝑄 ≥ 0. A primitive shift that repairs either negative
coordinate makes the other coordinate negative.
• If 𝑃 , 𝑄 > 0 , then 𝑢𝑣 − 𝑃 𝑢 − 𝑄𝑣 ∉ 𝑇 . Otherwise one would obtain a representation of 𝑢𝑣 with
both the 𝑢- and 𝑣-coefficients positive, whereas the integral solutions of 𝑋𝑢 + 𝑌 𝑣 = 𝑢𝑣 are exactly
𝑋 = 𝑣(1 − 𝑘), 𝑌 = 𝑢𝑘.
• If 0 ≤ 𝑃 < 𝑣, 0 ≤ 𝑄 < 𝑢 , then 𝑃 𝑢 + 𝑄𝑣 − 𝑢𝑣 ∉ 𝑇. In 𝑣-normal form, the 𝑢-coefficient is 𝑃 − 𝑣 < 0.
Moreover, if 0 < 𝐴 < 𝑣, 0 < 𝐵 < 𝑢, then
(𝐴𝑢 + 𝑇 ) ∩ (𝐵𝑣 + 𝑇 ) = (𝐴𝑢 + 𝐵𝑣 + 𝑇 ) ∪ (𝑢𝑣 + 𝑇 ). (2GI)
Indeed, write 𝑋 = (𝐴 + 𝑝)𝑢 + 𝑞𝑣 = 𝑟𝑢 + (𝐵 + 𝑠)𝑣with 𝑝, 𝑞, 𝑟, 𝑠 ≥ 0. If the primitive shift between
the two representations is zero, both coefficients contain 𝐴 and 𝐵. If it is positive, the first 𝑢-coefficient is
at least 𝑣; if it is negative, the 𝑣-coefficient in the second representation is at least 𝑢. Thus 𝑋 ∈ 𝑢𝑣 + 𝑇. The
reverse inclusion is immediate from 𝐴 < 𝑣, 𝐵 < 𝑢.
A.2 Branch I: the walk and the necessity of four hits
Let 𝜃 = 𝐿 = 𝑢 + 𝑣 + 𝑡0, where 𝑡0 = 𝑝0𝑢 + 𝑞0𝑣 ∈ 𝑇 . If 𝑘0 = 0, then 𝜇 = 𝐿 ∈ 𝑇 , so 𝑘0 = 1. Hence
𝜅 = 𝑑 − 𝑒, 1 ≤ 𝜅 ≤ 𝑑 − 1 − 𝑠, 𝑚 = 𝑑𝐿 − 𝜅𝑤, 𝐸 = 𝑤 − 𝐿 > 0.
The final positivity follows from 𝑚 < 𝑤 and 𝜅 + 1 ≤ 𝑑 − 𝑠 < 𝑑. Define
𝑐𝑛 = ⌈ 𝑛𝜅 − 𝑠
𝑑 ⌉ , 𝑗 𝑛 = 𝑠 − 𝑛𝜅 + 𝑑𝑐𝑛, 𝜏 𝑛 = 𝜌 + 𝑛𝐿 − 𝑐𝑛𝑤.
FS is equivalent to 𝜏𝑛 ∈ 𝑇 (𝑛 ≥ 1). Each adjacent step is either 𝐿 or 𝐿 − 𝑤. A return is an index with
𝑗𝑛 = 0, equivalently 𝑛𝜅 ≡ 𝑠 (mod 𝑑).
The 𝑇-coordinate of 𝑎𝑥 − 𝑦 + 𝑛𝑚is 𝜏𝑛 + 𝐴𝑢 − 𝑣. For 𝜏 in 𝑇,
𝜏 + 𝐴𝑢 − 𝑣 ∉ 𝑇 ⟺ 𝜏 ∈ 𝑈 𝑥 ∶= {𝑗𝑢 ∶ 0 ≤ 𝑗 < 𝐷}.
This follows directly from the normal form in § A.1. Although 𝑎𝑥 − 𝑦 ∈ 𝐻 , actual pseudo-Frobenius
behavior gives 𝑞𝑥 + 𝑦 ∈ Γ, while GAP-K yields 𝑎𝑥 − 𝑦 ∉ 𝐾. Hence a hit of 𝑈𝑥 is required for some 𝑛 ≥ 1.
Dually, a hit is required in
𝑈𝑦 = {𝑗𝑣 ∶ 0 ≤ 𝑗 < 𝐶}
Likewise, for 𝑎𝑥 − 𝑤 + 𝑛𝑚, every nonreturn index automatically gives an element of 𝐻. At a return, the
𝑇-coordinate becomes 𝜏𝑛 + 𝐴′𝑢 − 𝛽𝑣. This is a gap of 𝑇 precisely when the same 𝜏𝑛 lies in
75

PAGE 77
Canonical reduction and type T ype bound under canonical reduction
𝑍𝑥 = {𝑝𝑢 + 𝑞𝑣 ∶ 0 ≤ 𝑝 < 𝐷′, 0 ≤ 𝑞 < 𝛽}
Since 𝑎𝑥 − 𝑤 ∈ 𝐻 and 𝑞𝑥 + 𝑤 ∈ Γ, such a return hit is necessary. Dually, at a return one also needs a hit
in
𝑍𝑦 = {𝑝𝑢 + 𝑞𝑣 ∶ 0 ≤ 𝑝 < 𝛼, 0 ≤ 𝑞 < 𝐶′}
All four hits arise from the same actual pseudo-Frobenius pair and the same walk.
A.3 Branch I: the four hits are incompatible
Write 𝑎 = 𝐴′ > 0, 𝑏 = 𝐵′ > 0. Let 𝜌 = 𝑢𝑣 − 𝑤 − 𝑎𝑢 − 𝑏𝑣. At a return index 𝑁, one has 𝑐𝑁−1 = 𝑐𝑁 and
𝑐𝑁+1 = 𝑐𝑁 + 1. Thus, if 𝑁 > 1,
𝜏𝑁−1 = 𝜏𝑁 − 𝐿 ∈ 𝑇 .
If 𝜏𝑁 = 𝑝𝑢 + 𝑞𝑣 ∈ 𝑍𝑥 or 𝑍𝑦, then 𝑝 < 𝑣, 𝑞 < 𝑢. Hence § A.1 gives
𝑝 ≥ 𝑝0 + 1, 𝑞 ≥ 𝑞 0 + 1. (LOWER)
We do not impose this restriction when 𝑁 = 1.
If both the 𝑍𝑥- and 𝑍𝑦-hits occur at indices 𝑁 > 1 , then 𝑅 = 𝛼 − 𝑝0 − 1 > 0 and 𝑄 = 𝛽 − 𝑞0 − 1 > 0.
But 𝑐1 ∈ {0, 1}, and
𝜏1 = 𝑢𝑣 − (𝑎 + 𝑅)𝑢 − (𝑏 + 𝑄)𝑣 − 𝑐1𝑤 ∉ 𝑇
(after substituting the positive coefficient representation of 𝑤 and applying §A.1). Hence one of the return
hits must occur at 𝑁 = 1, and in particular 𝜅 = 𝑠. The base point 𝜏1 splits into the three cases X-only, Y -only,
and XY .
In general, a later hit 𝜏𝑁 = 𝑗𝑢 , 𝑗 < 𝐷 , in 𝑈𝑥 must occur through a carry. Without a carry, 𝜏𝑁−1 =
(𝑗 − 𝑝0 − 1)𝑢 − (𝑞0 + 1)𝑣 ∉ 𝑇. The dual statement holds for 𝑈𝑦.
X-only. The 𝑍𝑦-hit is later, so LOWER gives 𝑝0 ≤ 𝛼 − 2, 𝑞0 ≤ 𝑢 − 𝑏 − 2. Put 𝑅 = 𝛼 − 𝑝0 − 1 > 0 and
𝑆 = 𝑞0 + 1 − 𝛽. Then
𝑤 − 𝐿 = 𝑅𝑢 − 𝑆𝑣, 𝜏 1 = (𝑣 − 𝑎 − 𝑅)𝑢 + (𝑆 − 𝑏)𝑣.
Both coefficients lie below the upper edge of the fundamental strip, so FS implies 𝑆 ≥ 𝑏 . Also 1 ≤
𝑅 ≤ 𝛼 − 1, 1 ≤ 𝑆 < 𝑢 . For the base point to lie in 𝑈𝑥, one would need 𝑆 = 𝑏 , but then its 𝑢-coefficient
𝑣 − 𝑎 − 𝑅 ≥ 𝐷 + 1is too large. If there were a later 𝑈𝑥-hit, its carry predecessor would be (𝑗 + 𝑅)𝑢 − 𝑆𝑣;
however,𝑗 + 𝑅 ≤ 𝑣 − 𝑎 − 2 < 𝑣 and 0 < 𝑆 < 𝑢 , so this is a 𝑇-gap. Hence there is no 𝑈𝑥-hit.
Y-only. The same argument, with 𝑢 ↔ 𝑣, 𝐴 ↔ 𝐵, 𝛼 ↔ 𝛽, and 𝑎 ↔ 𝑏, shows that there is no 𝑈𝑦-hit.
XY. The coordinate representation in both rectangles is unique in the range 𝑝 < 𝑣, 𝑞 < 𝑢, so
𝜏1 = 𝑝𝑢 + 𝑞𝑣, 0 ≤ 𝑝 < 𝛼, 0 ≤ 𝑞 < 𝛽.
𝐸 = 𝑢𝑣 − (𝑎 + 𝑝)𝑢 − (𝑏 + 𝑞)𝑣, 𝜏2 = (2𝑝 + 𝑎)𝑢 + (2𝑞 + 𝑏)𝑣 − 𝑢𝑣.
If the 𝑈𝑥-hit occurs at the base point, then also𝑝 ≤ 𝐷−1, and hence 2𝑝+𝑎 ≤ (𝛼−1)+(𝐷−1)+𝑎 = 𝑣−2.
If a later hit is 𝑗𝑢with 𝑗 < 𝐷, then its carry predecessor is
𝑗𝑢 + 𝐸 = 𝑢𝑣 − (𝑎 + 𝑝 − 𝑗)𝑢 − (𝑏 + 𝑞)𝑣.
76

PAGE 78
Canonical reduction and type T ype bound under canonical reduction
The interior-gap criterion from §A.1 gives 𝑗 ≥ 𝑎+𝑝. Together with 𝑝 ≤ 𝛼−1, this again yields 2𝑝+𝑎 < 𝑣.
Dually, the 𝑈𝑦-hit forces 2𝑞 + 𝑏 < 𝑢. Therefore the final gap criterion in § A.1 gives 𝜏2 ∉ 𝑇 , contradicting
FS.
Thus the four hits are incompatible in all three cases, and Branch I is eliminated.
A.4 Branch II, 𝑘0 = 1: D-row drop or Branch I
Let 𝜃 = 𝜌 + 𝑢 + 𝑣 + 𝑡0, with 𝑡0 ∈ 𝑇 . If 𝑘0 = 1, then 𝑒 ≥ 𝑠 + 1, 𝜅 = 𝑑 − 𝑒 > 0, and
𝜇 = 𝜃 − 𝑤, 𝑚 = 𝑑𝜃 − 𝜅𝑤, 𝜅𝑤 < 𝑑𝜃 < (𝜅 + 1)𝑤.
Set 𝑔 = 𝑤 − 𝑢 − 𝑣 − 𝑡0 = 𝑤 − 𝜃 + 𝜌. We record explicitly that 𝑔 > 0: from 𝑟 > 0, 𝜌 > −𝑠𝑤/𝑑, and from
𝑚 < 𝑤, 𝜃 < (𝜅 + 1)𝑤/𝑑. Hence
𝜃 − 𝜌 < 𝜅 + 𝑠 + 1
𝑑 𝑤 ≤ 𝑤. (K1-g)
The backward points of the two D-rows are
𝑎′
𝑥 − 𝑚 = 𝜅𝑤 + 𝑑(𝐴′𝑢 + 𝑔), 𝑎 ′
𝑦 − 𝑚 = 𝜅𝑤 + 𝑑(𝐵′𝑣 + 𝑔).
If either lies in 𝐻, then it lies in 𝐾, and CAN gives the corresponding 𝑐′ − 𝑚 ∈ Γ, contradicting ALL-AP .
Thus both are gaps of 𝐻, so 𝐴′𝑢 + 𝑔, 𝐵′𝑣 + 𝑔 ∉ 𝑇 . By symmetry of 𝑇,
𝐹𝑇 − 𝑔 ∈ (𝐴′𝑢 + 𝑇 ) ∩ (𝐵′𝑣 + 𝑇 ).
Apply 2GI. Since 𝑔 > 0, one has 𝐹𝑇 − 𝑔 < 𝑢𝑣, so the 𝑢𝑣 + 𝑇 component is impossible. Therefore
𝐹𝑇 − 𝑔 − 𝐴′𝑢 − 𝐵′𝑣 = 𝜌 + 𝑡0 = 𝜃 − 𝑢 − 𝑣 ∈ 𝑇 .
This is precisely Branch I, already eliminated. Hence the case 𝑘0 = 1 is also impossible.
A.5 Branch II, 𝑘0 = 0, 𝑒 = 0
Here 𝜃 = 𝜇 ∉ 𝑇 and 𝑚 = 𝑑𝜃. By multiplicity, 0 < 𝜃 < min(𝑢, 𝑣). Since 𝑐1 = ⌊(𝑠 + 𝑒)/𝑑⌋ = 0, the first
stable coordinate is
𝜏1 = 𝜌 + 𝜃 = 2𝜃 − 𝑢 − 𝑣 − 𝑡0 < 0.
This contradicts FS.
A.6 Branch II, 𝑘0 = 0, 𝑒 > 0: cross-core exclusion
Write 𝑡0 = 𝑝𝑢 + 𝑞𝑣. Since 𝜃 = 𝜌 + 𝑢 + 𝑣 + 𝑡0 ∉ 𝑇 , one has 𝑝 ≤ 𝐴 − 2, 𝑞 ≤ 𝐵 − 2. Hence
𝑃 = 𝐴 − 1 − 𝑝 > 0, 𝑅 = 𝐵 − 1 − 𝑞 > 0, 𝑄 = 𝑢 − 𝑅, 𝑆 = 𝑣 − 𝑃 ,
𝜃 = −𝑃 𝑢 + 𝑄𝑣 = 𝑆𝑢 − 𝑅𝑣 < 0. (K0)
The final inequality follows from 𝑚 = 𝑒𝑤 + 𝑑𝜃 < 𝑤 and 𝑒 ≥ 1 . We have 𝑃 ≤ 𝐴 − 1, 𝑅 ≤ 𝐵 − 1 and
0 < 𝑄 < 𝑢, 0 < 𝑆 < 𝑣.
Let 𝑘 = 𝑑 − 𝑠 , 𝑐𝑛 = ⌊(𝑠 + 𝑛𝑒)/𝑑⌋, and 𝜏𝑛 = 𝜌 + 𝑛𝜃 + 𝑐𝑛𝑤 ∈ 𝑇 . If 𝑐1 = 0, then 𝜌 + 𝜃 < 0 , so 𝑒 ≥ 𝑘.
Put 𝐿0 = 𝑢 + 𝑣 + 𝑡 0. Then 𝜏1 = 2𝜃 + 𝑤 − 𝐿0 ≥ 0. With ℎ0 = −𝜃 > 0 , this gives 2ℎ0 ≤ 𝑤 − 𝐿0 < 𝑤.
Together with 𝑚 < 𝑤,

(𝑒 − 1)𝑤 < 𝑑ℎ0 < 𝑑𝑤/2, 2𝑒 ≤ 𝑑 + 1. (PHASE)
Let 𝜆𝑛 = ⌈𝑛𝑒/𝑑⌉and 𝜀𝑛 = 𝜆𝑛 − 𝑐𝑛 ∈ {0, 1}. If 𝑌𝑛 denotes the 𝑇-coordinate in the 𝐻-normal form of
𝑐𝑦 − 𝑛𝑚, direct calculation gives

By ALL-AP ,𝑐𝑦 − 𝑛𝑚 ∉ 𝐻 for every 𝑛 ≥ 1, hence 𝑌𝑛 ∉ 𝑇 .
On the other hand, the 𝑇-coordinate of 𝑎𝑥 − 𝑦 + 𝑛𝑚is 𝜏𝑛 + 𝐴𝑢 − 𝑣. If this were a 𝑇-gap, the translation
criterion from § A.2 would force 𝜏𝑛 = 𝑗𝑢, with 0 ≤ 𝑗 < 𝐷.
• If 𝜀𝑛 = 0, then COMP gives 𝑌𝑛 = (𝐷 − 𝑗)𝑢 ∈ 𝑇, a contradiction.
• Suppose 𝜀𝑛 = 1 . Write 𝑛𝑒 = 𝑞𝑑 + 𝑟 , 0 ≤ 𝑟 < 𝑑 . Then 1 ≤ 𝑟 < 𝑘 . Since 𝑒 ≥ 𝑘 , one has 𝑛 ≥ 2 .
Moreover,
(𝑛 − 1)𝑒 = (𝑞 − 1)𝑑 + (𝑑 + 𝑟 − 𝑒), 𝑑 + 𝑟 − 𝑒 ≥ 𝑘
by PHASE, because 𝑑 + 𝑟 − 𝑒 − 𝑘 ≥ 𝑑 + 1 − 𝑒 − 𝑘 ≥ 𝑑 + 1 − 2𝑒 ≥ 0 . Hence 𝑐𝑛−1 = 𝑐𝑛 = 𝑞. The
preceding stable point is
𝜏𝑛−1 = 𝜏𝑛 − 𝜃 = (𝑗 + 𝑃 )𝑢 − 𝑄𝑣.
But 𝑗 + 𝑃 ≤ 𝐷 − 1 + 𝐴 − 1 = 𝑣 − 2 and 0 < 𝑄 < 𝑢, so this is a 𝑇-gap, contradicting FS.
Therefore 𝑎𝑥 − 𝑦 + 𝑛𝑚 ∈ 𝐻for every 𝑛 ≥ 0, so 𝑎𝑥 − 𝑦 ∈ 𝐾. GAP-K then gives 𝑞𝑥 + 𝑦 ∉ Γ, contradicting
the actual pseudo-Frobenius consequence 𝑞𝑥 + 𝑦 ∈ Γ.
This eliminates every 𝑘0/𝑒-case in Branch II. Hence not all RA W4 rows can remain in the Apéry set, and
TYPE-BRIDGE yields
𝑡(Γ) ≤ 4.
B The relative lattice of three arms and the in-box path argument
In this appendix, 𝐶 denotes a local 3 × 3 matrix and is unrelated to the CORE-ROOT coefficient 𝐶.
B.1 Input and the socle matrix
Let 𝐻 = ⟨𝑛1, 𝑛2, 𝑛3⟩ be a nonsymmetric three-generated numerical semigroup, and let
Γ = ⟨𝑚, 𝑛1, 𝑛2, 𝑛3⟩, 0 < 𝑚 < min

We use the standard Herzog package with the following convention. All 𝑎𝑖, 𝑏𝑖 are positive integers, and
𝜌𝑖 = 𝑎𝑖 + 𝑏𝑖, 𝜌 1𝑛1 = 𝑏2𝑛2 + 𝑎3𝑛3, 𝜌 2𝑛2 = 𝑎1𝑛1 + 𝑏3𝑛3, 𝜌 3𝑛3 = 𝑏1𝑛1 + 𝑎2𝑛2.

B.2 The minimal factorization forced by three actual arms

Thus 𝑠 − 𝑝 ⋅ 𝑛 = 𝑘𝑚. No uniqueness of factorization is assumed at this stage.
B.3 Empty tetrahedron and the cyclic quotient
Let 𝐿𝑑 = {𝑧 ∈ ℤ3 ∶ 𝑧 ⋅ 𝑛 ≡ 0 (mod 𝑑)}. Since 𝑛 is primitive, [ℤ3 ∶ 𝐿𝑑] = 𝑑. Define
𝐶 = 𝑈 − 1𝑝𝑇 , 𝑐 𝑖 = 𝑈𝑖 − 𝑝
Then 𝐶𝑛 = 𝑘𝑚1, and by (UD) and the determinant lemma, det 𝐶 = 𝑘𝑚. Hence
rowℤ 𝐶 = 𝐿𝑘𝑚, 𝐿 𝑚/𝐿𝑘𝑚 ≅ ℤ/𝑘ℤ, [𝑧] ↦ 𝑧 ⋅ 𝑛/𝑚 ( mod 𝑘). (LAT)
The simplex Δ = conv(𝑝, 𝑈1, 𝑈2, 𝑈3)is empty with respect to the affine lattice 𝑝 + 𝐿𝑚. Indeed, if
𝑢 = (1 − 𝜏 )𝑝 + ∑
𝑖
𝜏𝑖𝑈𝑖, 𝜏 𝑖 ≥ 0, 𝜏 = ∑
𝑖
𝜏𝑖 ≤ 1
is a lattice point, then ℓ = (𝑠 − 𝑢 ⋅ 𝑛)/𝑚 = (1 − 𝜏 )𝑘is an integer.
• If 0 < ℓ < 𝑘 , then 𝑢 > 0 coefficientwise, so 𝑓 = ℓ𝑚 + ∑ 𝑖(𝑢𝑖 − 1)𝑛𝑖 is a genuine factorization,
contradicting minimality of 𝑘.
• If ℓ = 𝑘, then 𝑢 = 𝑝.
• If ℓ = 0 , then 𝑢 lies on the top face. At a nonvertex point at least two of the 𝑈𝑖 occur, and since each
row of (U) has its unique zero in a different coordinate, 𝑢 > 0 . Then 𝑓 = ∑ 𝑖(𝑢𝑖 − 1)𝑛𝑖 ∈ 𝐻 , a
contradiction.
Thus the only lattice points are the four vertices. The relative lattice volume is det 𝐶/[ℤ3 ∶ 𝐿𝑚] = 𝑘.

B.4 White classes: the only additional standard external theorem
The only result of White used here is the standard theorem that an empty lattice tetrahedron has lattice width
one: G. K. White, Lattice Tetrahedra*, Canad. J. Math. 16 (1964), 389–396. An exposition of the proof
is given by Khan–Rogers, An Exposition of White’s Characterization of Empty Lattice Tetrahedra*. The
quotient, height, and companion constructions below are proved within the present argument.
Assume 𝑘 ≥ 2. In the half-open parallelepiped, choose a representative of class 𝑗 ∈ {1, … , 𝑘 − 1}:
𝑟𝑗 = ∑
𝑖
𝜃𝑗,𝑖𝑐𝑖, 0 ≤ 𝜃 𝑗,𝑖 < 1
Emptiness gives ∑𝑖 𝜃𝑗,𝑖 > 1. For the negative class, the nonzero coordinates have coefficients 1 − 𝜃𝑗,𝑖.
Since both sums are > 1, all three coordinates are nonzero, and
0 < 𝜃𝑗,𝑖 < 1, 1 < ∑
𝑖
𝜃𝑗,𝑖 < 2.
Moreover, 𝑘 ∑𝑖 𝜃𝑗,𝑖 = 𝑟𝑗 ⋅ 𝑛/𝑚 ≡ 𝑗 (mod 𝑘), so
∑
𝑖
𝜃𝑗,𝑖 = 1 + 𝑗/𝑘. (AGE)
White’s width-one planes separate the vertices into two pairs. A 1 + 3split would have an empty triangular
facet that is unimodular in its own lattice and height one, forcing simplex volume one, contrary to 𝑘 ≥ 2 .
After a row permutation, the planes may therefore be taken to separate (𝑝, 𝑈1)from (𝑈2, 𝑈3). Normalize the
corresponding primitive affine functional to be linear with 𝑝 = 0 ; then 𝑐1 ↦ 0, 𝑐2, 𝑐3 ↦ 1 . Since 𝑟1 is a
lattice point, 𝜃1,2 + 𝜃1,3 is an integer in (0, 2), equivalently 0 < ⋅ < 2 for this quantity, and hence equals one.
By AGE, 𝜃1,1 = 1/𝑘.
Thus, for some 1 ≤ 𝑟 < 𝑘 ,
𝜃1 = (1/𝑘, 𝑟/𝑘, (𝑘 − 𝑟)/𝑘).
Nonvanishing of all coordinates in every class gives gcd (𝑟, 𝑘) = 1. Let 𝑞 < 𝑘 be the positive integer and
let ℓ be the nonnegative integer determined by 𝑟𝑞 = 1 + ℓ𝑘 . If the rows in the natural ordering are denoted
by 𝑅𝑖, then
𝑧 = 𝑅1 + 𝑟(𝑅2 − 𝑅3) − 𝑝
𝑘 ∈ ℤ 3, 𝑧 𝑞 = 𝑞𝑧 − ℓ(𝑅2 − 𝑅3) ∈ ℤ3. (Z)
Here 𝑟1 = 𝑐3 + 𝑧, 𝑟𝑞 = 𝑐3 + 𝑧𝑞. For 𝑣 = 𝑧 or 𝑧𝑞, the lower companions of class 𝑗 and 𝑘 − 𝑗 are
𝑝 + 𝑅3 − 𝑅1 + 𝑣, 𝑝 + 𝑅 3 − 𝑅2 + 𝑣, 𝑝 + 𝑣,
𝑅2 − 𝑣, 𝑅1 − 𝑣, 𝑅1 + 𝑅2 − 𝑅3 − 𝑣. (COMP)
They satisfy 𝑠 − 𝑢 ⋅ 𝑛 = (𝑘 − 𝑗)𝑚or 𝑗𝑚, respectively. Minimality therefore implies that none of the six
vectors is coefficientwise strictly positive . The argument is unchanged if classes coincide when 𝑞 = 1 or
𝑘 = 2.
B.5 Exact failure inequalities for color A
For 𝑈𝐴 in the natural ordering,
𝑧 = (−𝑋, 𝑌 , 𝑍), 𝑋 = 𝑟𝑏1 + 𝑝1
𝑘 , 𝑌 = 𝑏2 − (𝑟 − 1)𝑎2 − 𝑝2
𝑘 , 𝑍 = (𝑟 + 1)𝑎3 + 𝑟𝑏3 − 𝑝3
𝑘 .
The arm bounds give 𝑋 > 0, 𝑋 < 𝑝 1 + 𝜌1, 𝑋 < 𝑝1 + 𝑏1, −𝑎2 < 𝑌 < 𝑏 2, and 0 < 𝑍 < 𝜌 3. Apply failure
of the six vectors in COMP in the following order.

The final proof does not require a converse classification of the failure cone or its redundant inequalities.

B.8 White row ordering and conclusion
The row ordering selected by White may be arbitrary. Apply a simultaneous generator permutation that
returns the unique zero coordinate of each row to the corresponding index. An even permutation preserves
both 𝐴/𝐵and 𝑎/𝑏; an odd permutation exchanges 𝑎/𝑏and 𝐴/𝐵simultaneously. The arm box 𝑝𝑖 ≤ 𝑎𝑖 − 1 is
correspondingly carried to 𝑝′
𝑖 ≤ 𝑏′
𝑖 − 1. This can be checked directly by simultaneously permuting rows and
columns in (U). Thus the two natural master orderings exhaust all possibilities.
It follows that 𝑘 ≥ 2 is impossible, and therefore
𝑘 = 1, 𝑓 𝜀 = 𝑚 + ∑
𝑖
(𝑝𝑖 − 1)𝑛𝑖, 1 ≤ 𝑝 𝑖 ≤ 𝜆𝑖 ≤ 𝛼𝑖 − 1. (MINBOX)
This stage uses only lattice algebra and the minimal positive 𝑚-coefficient. No signed intermediate is
called actual. A lower companion used for contradiction is converted into a genuine factorization only when
all three coordinates are positive.
B.9 Exact interface to the 𝑘 = 1 dynamics
After color reversal if necessary, take color A. Put 𝑥 = 𝑝 ≥ 1, 𝑦 = 𝑎 − 𝑝 ≥ 1, 𝑧 = 𝑦 + 𝑏 ≥ 2. Then
𝐶 = 𝑈𝐴 − 1𝑝𝑇 = ⎛⎜⎜

−𝑥1 𝑧2 𝑦3
𝑦1 −𝑥2 𝑧3

⎞⎟⎟
⎠
, 𝐶𝑛 = 𝑚 1, det 𝐶 = 𝑚. (BOX)
The three-arm coordinate caps further imply that, for every 𝑢 ∈ ℤ3
>0 satisfying
𝑠 − 𝑢 ⋅ 𝑛 ∈ 𝑚ℤ≥0
we have 𝑢𝑖 ≤ 𝜆𝑖 ≤ 𝑎𝑖 − 1 = 𝑥𝑖 + 𝑦𝑖 − 1. This is the LOWER-BOX condition in § B.10.
For example, eliminating rows 1 and 3 of (BOX) gives, for Δ2 = 𝑥1𝑥3 − 𝑧1𝑦3,
Δ2𝑛3 = (𝑥1𝑦2 + 𝑧1𝑧2)𝑛2 − (𝑥1 + 𝑧1)𝑚 > 0

positive in the same way. Hence the positive minors needed downstream do not rely on an independent older
master lemma.
The matrix 𝐶, the box ℬ, and the local integers introduced here are used only in this appendix.
B.10 Exact input inherited from MINBOX
Let 𝑥𝑖, 𝑦𝑖, 𝑏𝑖 be positive integers, with 𝑧𝑖 = 𝑦𝑖 + 𝑏𝑖 and 𝑎𝑖 = 𝑥𝑖 + 𝑦𝑖. Assume

Let ℬ = ∏ 𝑖[1, 𝑎𝑖 − 1] ∩ ℤ3, with initial point 𝑝 = 𝑥.
Firing row 𝑖 means 𝑢 ↦ 𝑢 − 𝑐𝑖. A firing is positive* if all coordinates remain positive, and in-box* if the
result also lies in ℬ. Theorem DPE: a maximal in-box path from 𝑝 cannot terminate at a positivity sink; it
has a positive out-of-box firing.
Neither det 𝐶 = 𝑚 nor adj 𝐶1 = 𝑛 is an additional requirement for this dynamics theorem. INPUT is
already sufficient.

B.11 The finite unique in-box path
The three source regions for in-box firings are
𝐹1 ∶ 𝑢 1 ≤ 𝑦1 − 1, 𝑢2 ≥ 𝑧2 + 1, 𝑢3 ≥ 𝑦3 + 1,
𝐹2 ∶ 𝑢 1 ≥ 𝑦1 + 1, 𝑢2 ≤ 𝑦2 − 1, 𝑢3 ≥ 𝑧3 + 1,
𝐹3 ∶ 𝑢 1 ≥ 𝑧1 + 1, 𝑢2 ≥ 𝑦2 + 1, 𝑢3 ≤ 𝑦3 − 1.
These regions are pairwise disjoint. Every firing decreases 𝑢 ⋅ 𝑛by exactly 𝑚, so no path is infinite.
A state with no positive firing lies in the union of the following four regions; this follows by negating the
two positivity conditions for each row and expanding:
A ∶ 𝑢 1 ≤ 𝑦1, 𝑢2 ≤ 𝑧2; B ∶ 𝑢 1 ≤ 𝑧1, 𝑢3 ≤ 𝑦3;
C ∶ 𝑢 2 ≤ 𝑦2, 𝑢3 ≤ 𝑧3; D ∶ 𝑢 1 ≤ 𝑧1, 𝑢2 ≤ 𝑧2, 𝑢3 ≤ 𝑧3. (SINK)
The initial point 𝑝 is not a sink. If 𝑝 ∈ A, then 𝑐1+𝑐2 is coefficientwise nonnegative and its third coefficient
is 𝑦3 + 𝑧3 ≥ 3, giving 2𝑚 ≥ 3𝑛3, a contradiction. The cases B, C are the cyclic analogues. If 𝑝 ∈ D, the
sum of the three rows has coefficients 𝑦𝑖 + 𝑧𝑖 − 𝑥𝑖 ≥ 𝑦𝑖 ≥ 1, giving 3𝑚 ≥ 𝑛1 + 𝑛2 + 𝑛3, again impossible.
If the first firing is out of the box, the theorem is already proved. Otherwise, after cyclic normalization we
may assume 𝑝 ∈ 𝐹1. Then
𝑥1 < 𝑦1, 𝑥 2 > 𝑧2, 𝑥 3 > 𝑦3. (START)
We henceforth suppose, toward a contradiction, that a maximal path terminates at a sink.
B.12 Positive minors needed for the two-color calculation
Combining the first two rows of INPUT gives
(𝑥1𝑥2 − 𝑦1𝑧2)𝑛2 = (𝑦1𝑦3 + 𝑥1𝑧3)𝑛3 − (𝑥1 + 𝑦1)𝑚 > 0.
This follows from 𝑧3 ≥ 2, 𝑦3 ≥ 1, 𝑛3 > 𝑚. Hence Δ3 = 𝑥1𝑥2 − 𝑦1𝑧2 > 0.
Likewise,
(𝑥1𝑥3 − 𝑦3𝑧1)𝑛3 = (𝑧1𝑧2 + 𝑥1𝑦2)𝑛2 − (𝑧1 + 𝑥1)𝑚 > 0,
so Δ2 = 𝑥1𝑥3 − 𝑦3𝑧1 > 0. These inequalities establish the orientations of the two Farey corridors used
below.
B.13 Common arithmetic lemma for a two-color prefix
Consider only row 1 and row 𝑟, where 𝑟 = 2 or 3. Use the following common notation.
Symbol 𝑟 = 2 𝑟 = 3

A 𝑦1 𝑧1
B 𝑧2 𝑦3
v 𝑥2 𝑥3
𝑏 = 𝐴 − 𝑦 1 0 𝑏1
𝑐 = 𝐵 − 𝑦 𝑟 𝑏2 0
In either case 𝐴 > 𝑥, 𝑣 > 𝐵, and 𝐴/𝑥 < 𝑣/𝐵. Write the row counts as (𝑁 − 1, 𝑞 − 1), and define

Canonical reduction and type T ype bound under canonical reduction
𝑋 = 𝑞𝐴 − 𝑁 𝑥, 𝐷 = 𝑁 𝐵 − 𝑞𝑣.
The used coordinates are 𝑢1 = 𝐴 − 𝑋, 𝑢𝑟 = 𝐵 − 𝐷. For row 1 to fire in-box one needs 𝑋 ≥ 𝑏 + 1; for row
𝑟 to fire in-box one needs 𝐷 ≥ 𝑐 + 1. These conditions are sufficient once the remaining unused-coordinate
threshold is satisfied.
B.13.1 Successful crossings
At the 𝑞-th crossing where an in-box firing of row 𝑟 actually follows a run of row 1,
𝑁𝑞 = ⌈𝑞𝐴/𝑥⌉, 𝐸 𝑞 = 𝑁𝑞𝑥 − 𝑞𝐴, 𝑈 𝑞 = 𝑞𝑣 − (𝑁𝑞 − 1)𝐵,
1 ≤ 𝐸𝑞 ≤ 𝑥 − 𝑏 − 1, 1 ≤ 𝑈 𝑞 ≤ 𝐵 − 𝑐 − 1 = 𝑦𝑟 − 1. (RES)
Indeed, the in-box continuation by row 1 ends when 𝑋 ≤ 𝑏 . The strip 0 ≤ 𝑋 ≤ 𝑏 is an early-exit strip in
which switching to row 𝑟 is impossible. At a successful switch, 𝑋 = −𝐸 𝑞 < 0, while the preceding row-1
source satisfies 𝑋 + 𝑥 ≥ 𝑏 + 1, giving 𝐸𝑞 ≤ 𝑥 − 𝑏 − 1. The positivity and capacity of the row- 𝑟 source are
exactly the stated range for 𝑈𝑞.
Immediately after firing row 𝑟, one has 𝑢1 = 𝐸𝑞 < 𝑥 < 𝑦1, so row 𝑟 cannot fire again immediately. Hence
successful crossings occur as the chronological initial prefix 𝑞 = 1, 2, …. Before the third color appears, the
next move is a row-1 run.
RES gives
(𝑁𝑞 − 1)/𝑞 < 𝐴/𝑥 < 𝑣/𝐵 < 𝑁𝑞/𝑞 (CROSS)
Therefore, if all crossings through 𝑞 are successful, then for every 1 ≤ ℎ ≤ 𝑞 there is no integral fraction
𝑘/ℎ in the closed interval [𝐴/𝑥, 𝑣/𝐵]. This is the PREFIX separator rule ; it is a consequence, not an
additional assumption.
B.13.2 Injectivity, anti-chain property, and last-rank bound
For 𝑖 < 𝑗, set ℎ = 𝑗 − 𝑖, 𝑘 = 𝑁𝑗 − 𝑁𝑖. Then
𝐸𝑗 − 𝐸𝑖 = 𝑘𝑥 − ℎ𝐴, 𝑈 𝑗 − 𝑈𝑖 = ℎ𝑣 − 𝑘𝐵.
If the 𝐸-coordinates are equal, then 𝑘/ℎ = 𝐴/𝑥; if the 𝑈-coordinates are equal, then 𝑘/ℎ = 𝑣/𝐵. Either
contradicts PREFIX. If both coordinates increase, then 𝐴/𝑥 < 𝑘/ℎ < 𝑣/𝐵, again impossible.
Thus each coordinate is injective, and relative to the last point (𝐸𝑞, 𝑈𝑞), every earlier point satisfies 𝐸𝑖 >
𝐸𝑞 or 𝑈𝑖 > 𝑈𝑞. Counting integer slots gives
𝑞 + 𝐸𝑞 + 𝑈𝑞 ≤ (𝑥 − 𝑏) + (𝐵 − 𝑐) − 1. (LR)
In particular, 𝑞 ≤ 𝑥 − 𝑏 − 1 and 𝑞 ≤ 𝑦𝑟 − 1.
The same difference calculation applies to a terminal crossing 𝑞 = 𝑄 before success. If indices 1, … , 𝑄−1
are successful and the terminal point has 𝑁 = ⌈𝑄𝐴/𝑥⌉, then
𝑄 − 1 ≤ (𝑥 − 𝑏 − 1 − 𝐸)+ + (𝑦𝑟 − 1 − 𝑈 )+, (ELR)
provided the relevant terminal residues lie in their stated positive ranges. At every use of ELR below,
𝐸 ≥ 1, 𝑈 ≥ 1, and 𝐸 ≤ 𝑥 − 𝑏 − 1 are verified explicitly.

PAGE 87
Canonical reduction and type T ype bound under canonical reduction
B.14 Reciprocal-rank lemma, including preservation of the omitted prefix
Consider a state reached by ℓ ≥ 1 additional row-1 firings after the last successful crossing 𝑞 ≥ 1. Put
𝑄 = 𝑞 + 1, 𝑁 = 𝑁 𝑞 + ℓ, 𝑑 = 𝐴 − 𝑥 > 0, 𝑎 = 𝑣 − 𝐵 > 0, 𝐻 = 𝑁 − 𝑄.
Assume that at this state 𝑋 = 𝑄𝐴 − 𝑁 𝑥 ≥ 𝑏. Since Δ > 0, one has 𝐷 = 𝑁 𝐵 − 𝑄𝑣 < 0. Define
𝐸 = −𝐷 = 𝑄𝑎 − 𝐻𝐵, 𝑉 = 𝑑 − 𝑋 = 𝐻𝑥 − (𝑄 − 1)𝑑.
From RES and ℓ ≥ 1,
𝐻 ≥ 1, 1 ≤ 𝐸 ≤ 𝑎 − 1, 1 ≤ 𝑉 ≤ 𝑑 − 𝑏. (DR-BOX)
Indeed, 𝐸 = 𝑎 − ℓ𝐵 + 𝑈𝑞 ≤ 𝑎 − 𝑐 − 1, while 𝑋 = 𝐴 − 𝐸𝑞 − ℓ𝑥 ≤ 𝑑 − 1. Therefore
(𝑄 − 1)/𝐻 < 𝐵/𝑎 < 𝑥/𝑑 ≤ 𝑄/𝐻. (DR-BRACKET)
B.14.1 Complete proof of the dual PREFIX
Suppose that for some 𝑠 < 𝐻 there were a positive integer 𝑘 with 𝐵/𝑎 ≤ 𝑘/𝑠 ≤ 𝑥/𝑑 . Since 𝑠𝑥/𝑑 <
𝐻𝑥/𝑑 ≤ 𝑄, one has 𝑘 ≤ 𝑄 − 1 = 𝑞.
Taking reciprocals and adding one gives
𝐴/𝑥 ≤ (𝑘 + 𝑠)/𝑘 ≤ 𝑣/𝐵,
contradicting the original successful PREFIX at denominator 𝑘 ≤ 𝑞.
Thus, for 𝑠 = 1, … , 𝐻 − 1, the dual crossing index
𝐾𝑠 = ⌈𝑠𝐵/𝑎⌉ = ⌈𝑠𝑥/𝑑⌉
is defined, with dual residues
𝐸∗
𝑠 = 𝐾𝑠𝑎 − 𝑠𝐵 ∈ [1, 𝑎 − 1], 𝑉 ∗
𝑠 = 𝑠𝑥 − (𝐾𝑠 − 1)𝑑 ∈ [1, 𝑑 − 1].
For the terminal value 𝑠 = 𝐻, set 𝐾𝐻 = 𝑄 and use 𝐸, 𝑉 from DR-BOX. This convention is also consistent
at right-endpoint equality.
B.14.2 Preservation of the shifted gap
For 𝑠 < 𝐻, the corresponding original count pair is (𝑁𝑠, 𝐾𝑠) = (𝑠 + 𝐾𝑠, 𝐾𝑠). Its original 𝑋-coordinate is
𝑋𝑠 = 𝐾𝑠𝑑 − 𝑠𝑥 = 𝑑 − 𝑉 ∗
𝑠 ∈ [1, 𝑑 − 1].
Since 𝐾𝑠 ≤ 𝑄, the inequality 𝑋𝑠 > 0 gives 𝑁𝑠 < 𝐾𝑠𝐴/𝑥, while 𝑋𝑠 < 𝑑 gives 𝑁𝑠 > (𝐾𝑠 − 1)𝐴/𝑥 + 1.
Therefore 𝑁𝑠 is an integer count on the row-1 run between the original (𝐾𝑠 − 1)-st successful firing and the
𝐾𝑠-th crossing.
If 𝐾𝑠 < 𝑄, that run lies in the already successful PREFIX. If 𝐾𝑠 = 𝑄, then 𝑠 < 𝐻 gives 𝑁𝑠 < 𝑁 , so
the state lies before the terminal point on the current final row-1 run. In either case the path actually passes
through this state and continues with row 1.
When 𝐾𝑠 = 1, the “0-th successful firing” means the first row-1 run from the initial state. The inequalities
𝑁𝑠 > 1 and 𝑁𝑠 < 𝑁1 give the same correspondence directly, without assuming a nonexistent crossing.
Hence 𝑋𝑠 ≥ 𝑏 + 1, so
86

PAGE 88
Canonical reduction and type T ype bound under canonical reduction
𝑉 ∗
𝑠 ≤ 𝑑 − 𝑏 − 1 (𝑠 < 𝐻).
Thus the assertion that the dual residues avoid the same early-exit strip is established by explicit correspon-
dence with integer counts actually traversed by the path, not by formal analogy.
B.14.3 Dual rank
Applying the same difference calculation as in §B.13.2 to the dual PREFIX gives injectivity and the anti-chain
property. Hence
𝐻 − 1 ≤ (𝑎 − 1 − 𝐸) +max(𝑑 − 𝑏 − 1 − 𝑉 , 0).
If 𝑉 ≤ 𝑑 − 𝑏 − 1 , this directly gives the desired bound; if 𝑉 = 𝑑 − 𝑏 , the second term is zero. In either
case,
𝐻 + 𝐸 + 𝑉 ≤ 𝑎 + 𝑑 − 𝑏. (DR)
No infinite descent or new actual row is assumed in this lemma. It counts explicitly specified states in the
original finite prefix.
B.15 Rule for weighted certificates
For every nonzero nonnegative integer row vector 𝜆, if 𝑣 = 𝜆𝐶 ≥ 0 and |𝑣|1 ≥ |𝜆|1, then
|𝜆|1𝑚 = 𝑣 ⋅ 𝑛 > |𝑣|1𝑚 ≥ |𝜆|1𝑚,
which is impossible. We use the certificates below according to this rule.
For row counts 𝑐 = (𝑟, 𝑠, 𝑡), the universal identity is
(𝑐 +1)𝐶 = (𝑦1 + 𝑧1 − 𝑢1, 𝑦2 + 𝑧2 − 𝑢2, 𝑦3 + 𝑧3 − 𝑢3). (MC)
B.16 A one-color path cannot terminate at a sink
Suppose only row 1 has fired, 𝑁 − 1 ≥ 1 times. Then 𝑢1 = 𝑁 𝑥1, and the box condition gives (𝑁 − 1)𝑥1 ≤
𝑦1 − 1.
𝑁 ≤ 𝑦 1 − 𝑥1 + 1, 𝑁 (𝑥 1 + 1) ≤ 2𝑦1, 𝑦 1 + 𝑧1 − 𝑁 𝑥1 ≥ 𝑁 + 𝑏1.
The first inequality follows from (𝑥1 − 1)(𝑦1 − 𝑥1 − 1) ≥ 0.
For sink A, take 𝜆 = (𝑁 , 1, 0). Then 𝜆𝐶 = (𝑦1 − 𝑢1, 𝑧2 − 𝑢2, 𝑁 𝑦3 + 𝑧3) ≥ 0, and the third coefficient is
at least 𝑁 + 2 > |𝜆|.
For sink B, take 𝜆 = (𝑁 , 0, 1). Then 𝜆𝐶 = (𝑧1 − 𝑢1, 𝑁 𝑧2 + 𝑦2, 𝑦3 − 𝑢3) ≥ 0, and the second coefficient
is at least 2𝑁 + 1 ≥ |𝜆|.
For sinks C, D, use MC with 𝜆 = (𝑁 , 1, 1). The first coefficient is at least 𝑁 + 𝑏 1, and the remaining
two are at least (𝑧2, 𝑦3)or (𝑦2, 𝑦3), respectively. Thus 𝜆𝐶 ≥ 𝜆 componentwise. Every case contradicts the
weighted-certificate rule.
B.17 Excluding all terminal states of a {1, 2}two-color path
Let 𝑁 = 𝑟 + 1, 𝑄 = 𝑠 + 1, and set 𝑋 = 𝑄𝑦1 − 𝑁 𝑥1, 𝑌 = 𝑁 𝑧 2 − 𝑄𝑥2 − 𝑏2, 𝑢1 = 𝑦1 − 𝑋, 𝑢2 = 𝑦2 − 𝑌 .
87

PAGE 89
Canonical reduction and type T ype bound under canonical reduction
There have been 𝑞 = 𝑄 − 1 successful crossings, so 𝑄 ≤ 𝑥 1, 𝑄 ≤ 𝑦 2. From the box condition and
𝑞 ≤ 𝑥1 − 1, we also have 𝑁 ≤ 𝑦 1. For if 𝑁 ≥ 𝑦 1 + 1, then 𝑢1 = 𝑁 𝑥1 − 𝑞𝑦1 ≥ 𝑥1 + 𝑦1, contrary to the box
condition.
Sink A would require 𝑋 ≥ 0, 𝑌 ≥ −𝑏 2, but
𝑥2𝑋 + 𝑦1𝑌 = −𝑁 Δ3 − 𝑦1𝑏2 < −𝑦1𝑏2
which is impossible.
For sink D, take 𝜆 = (𝑁 , 𝑄, 1). By MC, 𝜆𝐶 = (𝑧1 + 𝑋, 𝑧2 + 𝑌 , 𝑦3 + 𝑧3 − 𝑢3). The D-conditions together
with 𝑁 ≤ 𝑦 1, 𝑄 ≤ 𝑦2 give 𝜆𝐶 ≥ 𝜆 componentwise.
B.17.1 The last firing is row 2
Immediately before the last firing we are at the last successful crossing 𝑞 = 𝑄 − 1 , with 𝑁 = 𝑁 𝑞, 𝐸 =
𝐸𝑞, 𝑈 = 𝑈𝑞, 𝐷𝑞 = 𝑧2 − 𝑈.
After firing, 𝑢1 = 𝐸 < 𝑥 1 < 𝑦 1 and 𝑢2 = 𝑥 2 + 𝑈 > 𝑧 2. At a sink, one must have 𝑢3 ≤ 𝑦 3, since
otherwise row 1 would be positive. Thus the sink is B.
For 𝜆 = (𝑁 , 𝑞, 1), all coefficients of 𝜆𝐶 are nonnegative, and the margin is
|𝜆𝐶| − |𝜆| = 𝑏1 + (𝑦1 − 𝑁 + 𝐷𝑞 − 𝐸) + (𝑦2 − 𝑞 − 1) + (𝑦3 − 𝑢3).
By LR, 𝐷𝑞 − 𝐸 ≥ 𝑞 − 𝑥 1 + 𝑏2 + 1. Since 𝑞 < 𝑥 1, 𝑁 − 𝑞 = ⌈𝑞(𝑦1 − 𝑥1)/𝑥1⌉ ≤ 𝑦 1 − 𝑥1. Hence the
second bracket is at least 𝑏2 + 1, and all other terms are nonnegative.
B.17.2 The last firing is row 1, sink B, 𝑋 ≥ 0 : the pre-crossing terminal case
Sink B means 𝑋 ≥ −𝑏 1, 𝑢3 ≤ 𝑦3. The subcase 𝑋 ≥ 0 includes states before the crossing.
Apply the reciprocal-rank lemma § B.14 with 𝐴 = 𝑦1, 𝐵 = 𝑧2, 𝑣 = 𝑥2, 𝑏 = 0, 𝑐 = 𝑏2. Since ℓ ≥ 1 row-1
firings follow the last successful crossing 𝑞 ≥ 1, all hypotheses hold.
With 𝐻 = 𝑁 −𝑄, 𝑎 = 𝑥2 −𝑧2, 𝑑 = 𝑦1 −𝑥1, 𝐸 = −(𝑌 +𝑏2), and 𝑉 = 𝑑−𝑋 , we have𝐻 +𝐸 +𝑉 ≤ 𝑎+𝑑.
For 𝜆 = (𝑁 , 𝑄 − 1, 1),
𝜆𝐶 = (𝑏1 + 𝑋, 𝑥2 + 𝑧2 + 𝑌 , 𝑦3 − 𝑢3) ≥ 0.
The second coefficient is nonnegative because the box condition𝑢2 ≤ 𝑥2+𝑦2−1 gives 𝑥2+𝑧2+𝑌 ≥ 𝑧 2+1.
The margin is
|𝜆𝐶| − |𝜆| = 𝑏1 + 𝑑 + 𝑥2 + 𝑦2 − (𝐻 + 𝐸 + 𝑉 ) − 2𝑄 + (𝑦3 − 𝑢3)
≥ 𝑏1 + 𝑧2 + 𝑦2 − 2𝑄 + (𝑦3 − 𝑢3) ≥ 𝑏1 + 𝑏2 > 0. (PRE-CROSS-PATCH)
Thus both the pre-crossing cases 𝑋 > 0 and 𝑋 = 0 are excluded.
B.17.3 The last firing is row 1, sink B, 𝑋 < 0
Here 𝑁 = 𝑁𝑄 and 𝐸 = −𝑋 ∈ [1, 𝑥1 − 1]. Put 𝐷 = 𝑌 + 𝑏2 = 𝑁 𝑧2 − 𝑄𝑥2. For the same 𝜆 = (𝑁 , 𝑄 − 1, 1),
the margin is
(𝑏1 − 𝐸) + (𝑥2 − 𝑁 + 𝐷) + (𝑦2 − 𝑄) + (𝑦3 − 𝑢3).
The first, third, and fourth terms are nonnegative. If 𝐷 ≥ 0 , then 𝑁 ≤ 𝑥 2, because 𝑄 ≤ 𝑦 2 < 𝑧 2 and
𝑁 = ⌈𝑄𝑦1/𝑥1⌉ < 𝑄𝑥2/𝑧2 + 1, so the second term is also nonnegative.
If 𝐷 < 0, put 𝑊 = −𝐷 > 0 . If some prior 𝑈𝑗 ≤ 𝑊, then with ℎ = 𝑄 − 𝑗, 𝑘 = 𝑁 − 𝑁𝑗 + 1,
88

PAGE 90
Canonical reduction and type T ype bound under canonical reduction
𝑘𝑥1 − ℎ𝑦1 = 𝐸 − 𝐸𝑗 + 𝑥1 > 0, ℎ𝑥 2 − 𝑘𝑧2 = 𝑊 − 𝑈𝑗 ≥ 0,
which is a PREFIX separator. Hence all prior 𝑈𝑗 > 𝑊, and injectivity gives 𝑄 + 𝑊 ≤ 𝑦2.
Also 𝑁 − 𝑄 ≤ 𝑥2 − 𝑧2 because 𝑄 < 𝑧2 and 𝑦1/𝑥1 < 𝑥2/𝑧2. Therefore
𝑥2 − 𝑁 ≥ 𝑧 2 − 𝑄 ≥ 𝑦2 − 𝑄 ≥ 𝑊 .
The second term is nonnegative as well. This is the required predecessor estimate.
B.17.4 The last firing is row 1, sink C
Since 𝑌 ≥ 0 , one has 𝑋 < 0 and 𝑁 = 𝑁 𝑄. Put 𝐸 = −𝑋 and 𝑈 = 𝑢 2 = 𝑧2 − (𝑌 + 𝑏2) > 0. Apply ELR
to all prior points.
If 𝑈 > 𝑦 2 − 1, then 𝑄 + 𝐸 ≤ 𝑥1; otherwise 𝑄 + 𝐸 + 𝑈 ≤ 𝑥1 + 𝑦2 − 1. In either case, 𝑄 ≤ 𝑥1 − 𝐸 + 𝑌 =
𝑥1 + 𝑋 + 𝑌 .
For 𝜆 = (𝑁 − 1, 𝑄, 1),
𝜆𝐶 = (𝑥1 + 𝑧1 + 𝑋, 𝑌 , 𝑧3 − 𝑢3) ≥ 0.
The sum of the first two coefficients is at least 𝑧1 + 𝑄 ≥ 𝑁 + 𝑄 + 𝑏 1. Hence |𝜆𝐶| ≥ |𝜆|. This exhausts
the sink regions for the {1, 2}-path.
B.18 Excluding all terminal states of a {1, 3}two-color path
Let 𝑁 = 𝑟 + 1, 𝑅 = 𝑡 + 1, and set 𝑋 = 𝑅𝑧1 − 𝑁 𝑥1, 𝑌 = 𝑁 𝑦 3 − 𝑅𝑥3, 𝑢1 = 𝑧1 − 𝑋, 𝑢3 = 𝑦3 − 𝑌 .
With 𝑞 = 𝑅 − 1 successful crossings, one has 𝑞 ≤ 𝑥1 − 𝑏1 − 1, 𝑞 ≤ 𝑦3 − 1, and 𝑅 ≤ 𝑦3.
The box condition also gives 𝑁 ≤ 𝑦 1. If 𝑁 ≥ 𝑦 1 + 1, then
𝑢1 − (𝑥1 + 𝑦1 − 1) ≥ 𝑏1(𝑏1 + 𝑦1 − 𝑥1 + 1) + 1 > 0.
Sink B would require 𝑋 ≥ 0, 𝑌 ≥ 0 , contradicting 𝑥3𝑋 + 𝑧1𝑌 = −𝑁 Δ2 < 0.
For sink D, take 𝜆 = (𝑁 , 1, 𝑅). By MC and 𝑁 ≤ 𝑦 1, 𝑅 ≤ 𝑦3, one has 𝜆𝐶 ≥ 𝜆 componentwise.
B.18.1 The last firing is row 3
Immediately after the last successful crossing 𝑞, 𝑢1 = 𝐸𝑞 < 𝑥1 < 𝑦1 and 𝑢3 = 𝑥3 + 𝑈𝑞 > 𝑦3. If this is a
sink, then 𝑢2 ≤ 𝑧2, so it lies in A.
For 𝜆 = (𝑁 , 1, 𝑞),
𝜆𝐶 = (𝑦1 − 𝐸𝑞, 𝑧2 − 𝑢2, 𝑦3 + 𝑧3 − 𝑈𝑞) ≥ 0.
Using LR, 𝑞 + 𝐸𝑞 + 𝑈𝑞 ≤ 𝑥1 − 𝑏1 + 𝑦3 − 1, together with 𝑁 − 𝑞 ≤ 𝑧1 − 𝑥1 and 𝑞 ≤ 𝑦3 − 1, the margin
is at least 𝑏3 + 1 > 0.
B.18.2 The last firing is row 1, sink A
Here 𝑋 ≥ 𝑏 1, 𝑢2 ≤ 𝑧2. Apply the reciprocal-rank lemma §B.14 with 𝐴 = 𝑧1, 𝐵 = 𝑦3, 𝑣 = 𝑥3, 𝑏 = 𝑏1, 𝑐 = 0.
Set 𝐻 = 𝑁 −𝑅, 𝑑 = 𝑧1 −𝑥1, 𝑎 = 𝑥3 −𝑦3, 𝐸 = −𝑌 , 𝑉 = 𝑑−𝑋 , and 𝐷∗ = 𝑎+𝑑−𝑏 1 −(𝐻 +𝐸 +𝑉 ) ≥ 0.
For 𝜆 = (𝑁 , 1, 𝑅 − 1),
𝜆𝐶 = (𝑋 − 𝑏1, 𝑧2 − 𝑢2, 𝑥3 + 𝑧3 + 𝑌 ).
89

PAGE 91
Canonical reduction and type T ype bound under canonical reduction
The first two coordinates are nonnegative by the A-conditions. For the third, using the residue 𝑈𝑞 at the
last success 𝑞 = 𝑅 − 1and 𝑁 = 𝑁𝑞 + ℓ, ℓ ≥ 1, 𝑥3 + 𝑧3 + 𝑌 = 𝑧 3 + (ℓ + 1)𝑦3 − 𝑈𝑞 > 0. Thus all coefficients
are nonnegative, and the exact margin is
|𝜆𝐶| − |𝜆| = 𝐷∗ + (𝑧2 − 𝑢2) + 𝑏3 + 2(𝑦3 − 𝑅) ≥ 𝑏3 > 0.
B.18.3 The last firing is row 1, sink C
Here 𝑢2 ≤ 𝑦2, 𝑌 ≥ −𝑏 3. Since the preceding state lies in 𝐹1, one has 𝑋 + 𝑥1 ≥ 𝑏1 + 1. Put
𝑃 = 𝑥 1 + 𝑋 − 𝑏1 − 1 ≥ 0, 𝐵 ∗ = 𝑏3 + 𝑌 ≥ 0, 𝐴 ∗ = 𝑥1 − 𝑏1 − 1, 𝐶 ∗ = 𝑦3 − 1.
Every successful index 𝑏1 < 𝑖 ≤ 𝑞 satisfies 𝐸𝑖 > 𝐴∗ − 𝑃 or 𝑈𝑖 > 𝐶∗ − 𝐵∗.
If both failed, then with ℎ = 𝑅 − 𝑖, 𝑘 = 𝑁 − 𝑁𝑖,
ℎ𝑧1 − 𝑘𝑥1 = 𝐸𝑖 + 𝑋 ≤ 0,
(𝑘 + 1)𝑦3 − ℎ𝑥3 = 𝑈𝑖 + 𝑌 ≤ 𝑦 3 − 𝑏3 − 1,
so 𝑧1/𝑥1 ≤ 𝑘/ℎ < 𝑥3/𝑦3, contradicting PREFIX for 1 ≤ ℎ ≤ 𝑞 .
Slot counting using injectivity gives𝑞−𝑏1 ≤ 𝑃 +𝐵∗ (trivial when 𝑞 ≤ 𝑏1). Thus 𝐷∗ = 𝑃 +𝐵∗−(𝑞−𝑏1) ≥
0.
For 𝜆 = (𝑁 −1, 1, 𝑅), the coefficients are (𝑥1 +𝑦1 +𝑋, 𝑦2 −𝑢2, 𝑏3 +𝑌 ). The first is positive by the row-1
predecessor condition 𝑋 + 𝑥1 ≥ 𝑏1 + 1, and the last two are nonnegative by the C-conditions. Therefore
|𝜆𝐶| − |𝜆| = (𝑦1 − 𝑁 ) + (𝑦2 − 𝑢2) + 𝐷∗ ≥ 0.
This excludes every two-color sink.
B.19 Excluding the first occurrence of the third color
The first firing is row 1. The first firing of a third color is therefore either {1, 2} → 3or {1, 3} → 2.
If row 3 first appeared immediately after row 2, the first coordinate of the preceding state would be at least
𝑦1 + 𝑧1 + 1 = 2𝑦1 + 𝑏1 + 1, whereas the box upper bound is 𝑥1 + 𝑦1 − 1, contradicting START 𝑥1 < 𝑦1.
The same argument excludes row 2 immediately after row 3. Hence in either case the third color first appears
immediately after row 1.
B.19.1 {1, 2} → 3
The source counts for the third color are (𝑁 − 1, 𝑄 − 1, 0). Substituting the preceding 𝐹1-condition and the
current 𝐹3-condition gives
1 − 𝑥1 ≤ 𝑋 ≤ −𝑏 1 − 1, 𝑧 2 + 1 − 𝑥2 ≤ 𝑌 ≤ −1, 1 ≤ 𝑢 3 ≤ 𝑦3 − 1.
Thus 𝑁 = 𝑁𝑄 and 𝐸 = −𝑋 ∈ [1, 𝑥1 − 1]. All prior indices 1, … , 𝑄 − 1are successful.
Every prior 𝐸𝑗 > 𝐸. Otherwise, with ℎ = 𝑄 − 𝑗, 𝑘 = 𝑁 − 𝑁𝑗, one would have 𝑘𝑥1 − ℎ𝑦1 = 𝐸 − 𝐸𝑗 ≥ 0.
Meanwhile 𝐷 = 𝑁 𝑧2 − 𝑄𝑥2 = 𝑌 + 𝑏 2 ≤ 𝑏2 − 1 and 𝐷𝑗 = 𝑧2 − 𝑈𝑗 ≥ 𝑏2 + 1, so 𝑘𝑧2 − ℎ𝑥2 = 𝐷 − 𝐷𝑗 < 0,
producing a PREFIX separator.
Hence 𝑄 + 𝐸 ≤ 𝑥1. Together with 𝑁 − 𝑄 ≤ 𝑦1 − 𝑥1, this gives 𝑁 + 𝐸 ≤ 𝑦1.
Also 𝑦2 + 𝐷 ≥ 𝑄. If 𝐷 ≥ 0, this follows from 𝑄 ≤ 𝑦2. If 𝐷 < 0, the same difference calculation as in
§B.17.3 shows that all prior 𝑈𝑗 > −𝐷, hence 𝑄 − 𝐷 ≤ 𝑦2.
For 𝜆 = (𝑁 , 𝑄, 1),
90

PAGE 92
Canonical reduction and type T ype bound under canonical reduction
𝜆𝐶 = (𝑧1 − 𝐸, 𝑦2 + 𝐷, 𝑦3 + 𝑧3 − 𝑢3) ≥ (𝑁 + 𝑏1, 𝑄, 𝑧3 + 1) ≥ 𝜆.
This is a contradiction.
B.19.2 {1, 3} → 2
The source counts are (𝑁 − 1, 0, 𝑅 − 1). The preceding 𝐹1-condition and the current 𝐹2-condition give
𝑏1 + 1 − 𝑥1 ≤ 𝑋 ≤ 𝑏 1 − 1, 1 ≤ 𝑢 2 ≤ 𝑦2 − 1, 𝑦 3 + 1 − 𝑥3 ≤ 𝑌 ≤ −𝑏 3 − 1.
Set 𝑃 = 𝑏 1 − 𝑋 ≥ 1 and 𝑊∗ = −𝑏3 − 𝑌 ≥ 1 . All prior indices 1, … , 𝑅 − 1are successful.
Every prior 𝐸𝑗 > 𝑃 . Otherwise, with ℎ = 𝑅 − 𝑗, 𝑘 = 𝑁 − 𝑁𝑗, one has 𝐷1 = ℎ𝑧1 − 𝑘𝑥1 = 𝐸𝑗 + 𝑋 ≤ 𝑏 1.
If 𝐷1 ≤ 0, then 𝑘/ℎ ≥ 𝑧1/𝑥1. On the other hand, (𝑘 + 1)𝑦3 − ℎ𝑥3 = 𝑈𝑗 + 𝑌 ≤ 𝑦 3 − 𝑏3 − 2 < 𝑦 3, so
𝑘/ℎ < 𝑥3/𝑦3, contradicting PREFIX.
If 1 ≤ 𝐷 1 ≤ 𝑏 1, the existence of a successful prefix gives 𝑏1 ≤ 𝑥 1 − 2, while 𝑘 = ⌊ℎ𝑧 1/𝑥1⌋ and
𝑁ℎ = 𝑘 + 1 . Therefore 𝐸ℎ = 𝑥 1 − 𝐷1 ≥ 𝑥 1 − 𝑏1, directly contradicting the successful EBOX bound
𝐸ℎ ≤ 𝑥1 − 𝑏1 − 1 at index ℎ < 𝑅. This is the integral form of the early-exit strip.
Next, every prior 𝑈𝑗 > 𝑊∗. Otherwise, the already-proved inequality 𝐸𝑗 > 𝑃 gives 𝑏1 +1 ≤ 𝐷1 ≤ 𝑥1 −2.
With 𝑘′ = 𝑘 + 1,
𝑘′𝑥1 − ℎ𝑧1 > 0, ℎ𝑥 3 − 𝑘′𝑦3 = 𝑊∗ − 𝑈𝑗 + 𝑏3 > 0,
again producing a PREFIX separator.
By injectivity, 𝑅 + 𝑃 ≤ 𝑥 1 − 𝑏1 and 𝑅 + 𝑊∗ ≤ 𝑦 3. Since the preceding state is a row-1 source, 𝑁 ≤
⌈𝑅𝑧1/𝑥1⌉. From 𝑅 < 𝑥1 and 𝑧1 = 𝑥1 + 𝑑, we get 𝑁 − 𝑅 ≤ 𝑑, hence 𝑁 + 𝑃 ≤ 𝑦 1.
For 𝜆 = (𝑁 , 1, 𝑅),
𝜆𝐶 = (𝑧1 − 𝑃 , 𝑦2 + 𝑧2 − 𝑢2, 𝑦3 − 𝑊∗) ≥ (𝑁 + 𝑏1, 𝑧2 + 1, 𝑅) ≥ 𝜆,
again a contradiction.
B.20 Exhaustion and connection to the upstream argument
Suppose that a finite maximal in-box path terminates at a sink.
• If only row 1 occurs, § B.16 gives a contradiction.
• If exactly two colors occur, § B.17–§B.18 give a contradiction.
• If all three colors occur, the first firing of the third color contradicts § B.19.
Therefore the endpoint of every maximal path admits a positive out-of-box firing.
This proves DPE, which applies directly to the matrix INPUT obtained from MINBOX with 𝑘 = 1.
B.20.1 Final connection to the same actual 𝑓𝜀
By §B.2, for the same semigroup and the same socle element under the assumption of three actual arms,
𝑓𝜀 = 𝑚 + ∑
𝑖
(𝑝𝑖 − 1)𝑛𝑖, 𝑞 𝑖 = 𝑓𝜀 − 𝜆𝑖𝑛𝑖 ∈ 𝑃 𝐹 (Γ), 1 ≤ 𝑝𝑖 ≤ 𝜆𝑖 ≤ 𝛼𝑖 − 1
First consider color A, so 𝛼𝑖 = 𝑎𝑖 and 𝑝 = 𝑥.
After a total of 𝑡 firings, write the state as 𝑢 = 𝑝 − 𝑟𝐶, with |𝑟|1 = 𝑡. Since 𝐶𝑛 = 𝑚1,
91

PAGE 93
Canonical reduction and type T ype bound under canonical reduction
𝑓𝐴 = (𝑡 + 1)𝑚 + ∑
𝑖
(𝑢𝑖 − 1)𝑛𝑖. (SAME-F)
At a positive state, every𝑢𝑖 ≥ 1, so this is a genuine factorization of the same 𝑓𝐴. The original Γ, 𝑚, 𝑛𝑖, 𝑓𝐴
have not changed.
The first positive out-of-box state 𝑢∗ supplied by DPE satisfies the same identity. Since it leaves ℬ =
∏𝑖[1, 𝑎𝑖 − 1] while all coordinates remain positive, some 𝑖 satisfies 𝑢∗
𝑖 ≥ 𝑎𝑖. Hence
𝑢∗
𝑖 − 1 ≥ 𝑎𝑖 − 1 ≥ 𝜆𝑖.
Removing 𝜆𝑖𝑛𝑖 from this one final coefficient vector gives
𝑞𝑖 = (𝑡 + 1)𝑚 + (𝑢∗
𝑖 − 1 − 𝜆𝑖)𝑛𝑖 + ∑
𝑗≠𝑖
(𝑢∗
𝑗 − 1)𝑛𝑗 ∈ Γ,
contradicting the fact that𝑞𝑖 is an actual pseudo-Frobenius gap. No semantic subtraction of a signed relation
is used.
Color B is carried to color A by an odd generator permutation together with the complete simultaneous
exchange of 𝑎/𝑏 and A/B. The three actual pseudo-Frobenius elements and their arm bounds are exchanged
at the same time, so the same conclusion holds.
Thus three actual arms of the same color cannot occur:
THREE-ARM CLOSED.
For either color, every actual row is either the corner 𝑓𝜀 or an arm 𝑓𝜀 − 𝜆𝑛𝑖. T wo distinct depths in the
same direction contradict pseudo-Frobenius comparability. A corner cannot coexist with an arm of the same
color for the same reason. Therefore, three rows of one color would have to consist of one arm in each of the
three directions, and this has just been excluded. Hence, within the canonical six-arm classification,
COLOR-CAP ≤ 2.
The chain MINBOX → DPE → SAME-F → THREE-ARM → COLOR-CAP requires no additional up-
stream lemma.
Proposition B.1 (THREE-ARM / COLOR-CAP). In the setting of the canonical six-arm classification, three
actual arms of the same color do not exist. Moreover, for each color there are at most two actual corner/arm
rows.
Proof. The first assertion is exactly THREE-ARM CLOSED, proved in § B.1–§B.20.1. The second is the
already-proved COLOR-CAP conclusion obtained at the end of §B.20.1 from incomparability and uniqueness
of depth in a fixed direction. No new hypothesis or conclusion is introduced here.

The following are the exact coefficients in POS-DECOMP . All are nonnegative, and in fact positive, for

+ 𝜂(𝑡2𝑧2 + 𝑡2𝑧 + 2𝑡𝑧2 + 2𝑡𝑧 + 𝑡 + 𝑧2 + 𝑧)

Expanding with respect to 𝑎𝑗 − 1, 𝑔 − 1, 𝑞′ gives the displayed identity. Expanding the explicit formulas
in this appendix provides a direct check.
D Specification and reproducibility of the static polynomial certificates
The polynomial identities used in the main text are stored as finite complete coefficient tables. These are not
outputs of a finite scan of numerical semigroups.

D.1 The 𝑖-fit for linear 𝒟
For Φ(𝑎) = ̂ 𝑚 − 𝒥and 𝑎∗ = 𝑓𝐵 + 𝑟 defined in § 9.11–§9.12,
Φ(𝑎∗) = 35 + ∑
𝛾≠0
𝑐𝛾y𝛾, 𝑐 𝛾 ≥ 0,
y = (𝑓 − 1, 𝑟, 𝛿 − 1, 𝛽 − 1, 𝑔 − 1, 𝜐, 𝛼 − 1, 𝑤, 𝜃 − 1, 𝜖, 𝑘 − 1, 𝑎𝑗 − 1, 𝑏𝑘 − 1).
All variables are nonnegative. All 715 monomials are contained in the static coefficient table . Each entry
in terms has a coefficient and an exponent vector, with exponents listed in the order specified by vari-
ables. The JSON names f,delta,beta,g,alpha,theta,k,a_j,b_k represent the corresponding shifted
variables with one subtracted. The variables r,u,w,eps are unshifted, with 𝑢 = 𝜐 and 𝑒𝑝𝑠 = 𝜖. Summing
all listed monomials reproduces the defining expression in the main text.

𝒫 = 63 + ∑

The shifts of all variables are defined in § 10.8, and the variable order is
p0, q0, s0, t0, r0, delta0, beta0, aj0, g0, alpha0, bk0, nu0, h0, z0, x0, w0
All 3,234 monomials, total degree 7, and constant term 63 are stored in the static coefficient table . Since
every nonconstant coefficient is also nonnegative, the positivity conclusion follows from this explicit finite
polynomial identity.

The bytes of the static tables and the mathematical computations performed by the verifiers are unchanged
from the adopted source. Only an obsolete OPEN status line at the end of the linear verifier was replaced there

respective defining expressions and compare every coefficient with the static tables. In ordinary use they
read the existing tables and do not regenerate them. The following commands are run from the root of the

python3 ../supplement/verification_scripts/LINEAR/verify_D_linear.py
python3 ../supplement/verification_scripts/EUCLIDEAN/verify_euclidean_closure.py
Python 3 and SymPy are required. Successful verification is an auxiliary consistency check. The actu-
ality statements, scope conditions, choice of the first point, parameter signs, and proof of termination are
established in the manuscript itself.
