D.4 The two defining expressions and evaluation procedures
For the first table, use the notation of § 9.11–§9.12:
𝐵 = 𝑘(𝑟 + 𝛿) + (𝑟 + 𝛽), 𝑎∗ = 𝑓𝐵 + 𝑟, 𝑑 = 𝑎 + 𝑘(𝑟 + 𝛿), 𝑆 = 𝑘𝑄 + 𝑔 + 𝜐
and substitute the formulas for 𝑎𝑖, 𝑏𝑖, 𝜌𝑗, 𝜌𝑘 from the same subsection into
Φ(𝑎) = −𝑑(𝜌𝑗𝜌𝑘 − 𝑎𝑗𝑏𝑘) + 𝑆(𝑎𝑖𝜌𝑘 + 𝑏𝑖𝑏𝑘) + 𝐶(𝑏𝑖𝜌𝑗 + 𝑎𝑖𝑎𝑗) − (𝑎𝑖𝜌𝑘 + 𝑏𝑖𝑏𝑘)
Then set 𝑎 = 𝑎∗, 𝑄 = 𝑓(𝑎𝑗 + 𝑔 + 𝜐) + 𝜃, 𝐶 = 𝛼 + 𝑓𝜒 + 𝑤, and 𝜒 = 𝑏𝑘 + 𝛼 + 1 + 𝜖. This is the defining
polynomial represented by the 715-term table, and its constant term is 35. The nonnegative parameter range
used in the proof is stated in § 9.12.
For the second table, use the definitions from § 10.7–§10.8 and evaluate
Φ(𝑃 ) = (𝐸𝐴 + 𝜃𝐵)𝜌𝑘 − 𝑎𝑗(𝜒𝐴 + 𝐻𝑝𝐵) − (𝜃𝜒 − 𝐸𝐻𝑝)(𝑃 − 𝛿) − [(𝑃 − 𝛿)𝜌𝑗 + (𝑃 − 𝛽)𝑎𝑗]
at 𝑃 = 𝐵 + 𝜈𝐴 . The quantities 𝐴, 𝐵are determined from the source matrix, with 𝜌𝑗 = 𝐿𝐸 + 𝑀 𝜃 − 𝑎𝑗
and 𝜌𝑘 = 𝐿𝜒 + 𝑀 𝐻𝑝 − 𝑏𝑘. At a terminal state, substitute
𝜃 = ℎ + 𝑧, 𝐸 = 𝜈(ℎ + 𝑧) + 𝑅 − ℎ, 𝐻 𝑝 = 𝑇 + 𝑤, 𝜒 = 𝜈(𝑇 + 𝑤) − 𝑇 + 1 + 𝑥
Expanding this expression in the following 16 variables yields the table of 3,234 terms, total degree 7,
and constant term 63. The determinant-one identity is used when identifying the expression with the actual
formula for 𝑚, but no additional determinant constraint is needed on the nonnegative-variable domain of the
expanded polynomial.
Both evaluation points are algebraic thresholds. Neither is assumed itself to satisfy the pseudo-Frobenius
or first-point conditions of an actual semigroup; the lower bounds for the actual parameters are obtained by
the monotonicity and multiplicity comparisons in the main text.

D.7 Required proof data and ordinary verification
The complete coefficient tables and verifiers are included in the reader-facing publication supplement. Ex-
act manuscript-to-certificate mappings are recorded in MANUSCRIPT_CERTIFICATE_MAP.md. Their original
bytes are retained. In ordinary verification, each table is read and compared coefficient-by-coefficient with
an independently expanded defining expression. The certificate-writing option is not used. Since the veri-
fiers regenerate result JSON files in the same supplementary directories, preservation of a distribution master
should be checked on a working copy of the package. From that working-copy root, run
python3 ../supplement/verification_scripts/LINEAR/verify_D_linear.py
python3 ../supplement/verification_scripts/EUCLIDEAN/verify_euclidean_closure.py
The Python and SymPy versions, expected outputs, and SHA-256 hashes of the checked files are recorded
in the publication supplement. Logically, the proof uses the finite identities and their complete coefficient
tables specified here; a program’s success message is not taken as an axiom. Computer assistance is used
for the practical coefficientwise verification of the large expansions. The scope conditions, nonnegative
representations, existence of the first point, and termination of the descent are proved in the manuscript. No
unverified attribution concerning the discovery process is made.
96

PAGE 98
Canonical reduction and type T ype bound under canonical reduction
Supplementary symbolic-verification files
A reader-facing reproducibility supplement accompanies this manuscript. It contains the complete static co-
efficient tables for the two large polynomial positivity identities, the unchanged symbolic verification scripts,
expected outputs, environment notes, SHA-256 checksums, and a map from the relevant manuscript locations
to the certificate files. The mathematical proof is self-contained in the article; these files provide practical
coefficientwise checks and are not a finite numerical search or a proof-assistant formalization. The reader-
facing verification supplement is supplied with this submission.

This research received no external funding.
Declaration of competing interest
The author declares no competing interests.
Data and code availability
No empirical dataset was used in this work. Symbolic verification scripts and complete static coefficient
certificates supporting the two large polynomial expansions are supplied with the publication supplement.
These files verify finite symbolic identities coefficient by coefficient; the mathematical argument is not based
on a finite numerical search of numerical semigroups and is not claimed to be formally verified in a proof
assistant. The reader-facing verification supplement is supplied with this submission.
Use of AI tools in the research process
During exploratory research, the author used ChatGPT (OpenAI) and Claude (Anthropic) as non-authoritative
assistants for brainstorming proof routes, adversarial proof checking, organizing literature searches, and sup-
port around symbolic-verification workflows. AI output was not treated as mathematical authority: mathe-
matical statements, proofs, bibliographic claims, and symbolic certificates remain subject to author verifica-
tion and approval. Specific model/version details can be supplied from the retained project records if required
by the journal.
Declaration of generative AI and AI-assisted technologies in the manuscript
preparation process
During the preparation of this work, the author used ChatGPT (OpenAI) and Claude (Anthropic) to assist
with literature-search organization, translation and language editing, document organization, and submission-
package preparation. After using these tools/services, the author reviewed and edited the content as needed
and takes full responsibility for the content of the published article.
Author contributions
Keisuke Sato: Conceptualization, Formal analysis, Investigation, Methodology, Validation, Writing – original
draft, Writing – review & editing.
